## 👤 User

Read TODO and give an idea of where we're at and what's next.
To my mind the biggest conundrum is seeding - single-face seeds aren't working well and it's unclear why - perhaps because we're just exploring boundary faces and good seeds require an interior face. However, a brute-force search of all faces for seeds is computationally expensive. This is effectively a search problem. Doing a random search or a recursive subdivision search could result in finding good seeds much faster, but doesn't improve the worst-case when a nurbs surface is required. However, given that all vertices should lie precisely on the surface, it should be possible to fit surfaces using a decimated set of vertices. I'm wondering if some kind of recursive subdivision could identify surfaces quickly and bound the worst case? I'm imagining something like: for a given set of contiquous faces to try to fit a surface to: try to fit a surface to a subset of the vertices that includes some vertices at the corners and the interior of the patch; if there's a fit then retry with all vertices in the patch and if there's a perfect fit select the surface; if there's no fit but it's plausible there's a fit within then subdivide the patch and retry on the sub-patches; if there's clearly no fit then give up. I'm not sure how to distinguish between the two latter cases reliably. Read the code for surface fitting and stage 2, and think about whether my algorithm idea might have merit.

```
94	  **The framing.** The assumption is that a B-rep face boundary is always a
95	  chain of mesh edges, tangent junctions included. There are two ways to use
96	  it, with very different risk: as a **constraint** ("the answer's boundaries
97	  must be mesh-edge chains" — provenance-independent, true for every faceter)
98	  or as a **detector** ("here is where the seams are" — provenance-dependent,
99	  weak on OCCT's Delaunay interiors, information-free on mesh-native input).
100	  **Stage 2 currently uses it as neither.** Stage 3 uses it fully, and is the
101	  proof of concept: §3.x "shared-edge topological assembly instead of geometric
102	  sewing" *deleted* a heuristic-laden alternative outright.
103

105	  price of not knowing the tangent seams. If the partition were known, each

107	  already exists and whose few constants are cost-control — subsampling, refit
108	  rounds — not decision thresholds). The evidence is already in hand: **sharp-
109	  edged models pass the ladder with 0 competition regions and no tuning at

157	
158	  Every remaining \`--compare\` failure has been a boundary sitting one
159	  tessellation row off, and nothing in the pipeline reasons about where a
160	  boundary *should* be — it falls out of flood plus claim resolution. The
161	  assumption guarantees the correct boundary **exists** as a mesh-edge chain,
162	  so this is selection from a guaranteed-nonempty set, not a search.
163	
164	  - Once two adjacent surfaces are committed, compute the curve where they
165	    meet, then reassign the boundary faces by which side of it they fall on,
166	    choosing the mesh-edge chain that best follows it.
167	  - **The hard case is exactly the one that matters.** At a *tangent* junction
168	    the surfaces graze rather than cross, so \`GeomAPI_IntSS\` is the wrong tool
169	    — it is numerically fragile there and that fragility is what produced the
170	    original 4.4e-2 mm error (a plane offset δ moves its intersection with a
171	    torus it should graze by √(2rδ), so tiny fit error is hugely amplified).
172	    For tangency, find the locus of minimum distance between the two fitted
173	    surfaces instead — for a plane grazing a torus that is the tangency circle,
174	    computable in closed form from the fitted parameters. Consider handling
175	    tangent and transversal junctions by different code paths; stage 3.2
176	    already classifies edges as tangent, and stage 3's compare already exempts
177	    tangent edges to the chordal tolerance for this reason.

200	  The corpus's STLs are binary, so vertices are float32 and the floor on vertex

202	  **1.96e-5 mm for stepped_block_toroidal**, matching their measured stage-1

206	  - **DO NOT make the acceptance tolerance scale with it — measured wrong.**
207	    Sweeping \`--vertex-tolerance\` on stepped_block_toroidal fine gives 138 / 87

211	  - **DO use it as the unit for fit quality.** Committed analytic surfaces fit
212	    their support to under ~1 quantum across a 7× range of quanta (medians
213	    0.31–0.47, maxima 0.6–1.2), while the spurious fits \`hill_with_hole\` used
214	    to commit sat at 80×. That is the scale-free commit criterion the
215	    post-refit tightening idea needs, and it is what "a genuine fit explains
216	    its support to the precision the data has" actually means.
217	  - **DO clamp and report.** A \`--vertex-tolerance\` below the quantum is
218	    unsatisfiable — the data does not carry that precision. Clamp, warn, and
219	    print the quantum in the stage-1 summary next to the existing vertex and
220	    centroid compare numbers.
221	  - **Not a flag.** The quantum is a measurable property of the input file, not
222	    a user preference; a flag would only let someone misdeclare it.
223	    \`--vertex-tolerance\` stays the user's *request*, the quantum is what the
224	    data can *support*, and the effective value is the larger of the two.
225	  - Note the quantum depends on distance from the origin, so the same part
226	    authored 200 mm off-origin carries ~10× worse data than one centred. Worth
227	    saying in the README: recentring before export improves precision, and
228	    nothing downstream can recover it afterwards.

250	
251	  **B. Estimate \`--surface-tolerance\` and \`--angular-tolerance\` from the mesh
252	  instead of defaulting them.** Both are stand-ins for settings the *exporter*
253	  used and then discarded; neither is a property of the fitting problem.
254	  \`--vertex-tolerance\` is different — it is a genuine precision request from
255	  the user and should stay a plain flag.
256	
257	  *Why it is measurable without a reference STEP.* The mesh is its own
258	  reference, because **vertices lie on the true surface (~1e-6 mm) while
259	  centroids sit inside it by the chordal sag**. For a facet whose vertices lie
260	  on a surface of radius R, with circumradius ρ perpendicular to the axis:
261	  \`depth = R − √(R² − ρ²) ≈ ρ²/(2R)\`. The tessellator sized the facet so this
262	  stays under its deflection setting ε, so the sag distribution over the mesh
263	  *is* a sample of ε.
264	
265	  *How far off the default is:* stage 1's \`--compare\` already reports the true
266	  sag — **8.9e-4 mm** on stepped_block_toroidal fine, **9.5e-4 mm** on rounded_cube
267	  fine — against a default of **0.4 mm**, i.e. ~400× too loose. (0.4 was a hand
268	  edit in March 2026 with no recorded rationale; see the tolerance-history
269	  notes.) Earlier measurements: worst centroid sag 0.026 mm Onshape-coarse,
270	  0.038 mm Fusion-low.
271	
272	  Three estimators, increasingly independent:
273	  1. **Fit-based, bootstrapped from the unambiguous regions.** Sharp-bounded
274	     regions are solved by the ladder with no tolerance choices beyond vertex
275	     validation. For each of their facets, distance from centroid to its own
276	     committed fit is a direct sag sample. High quantile → ε. Available before
277	     competition runs.
278	  2. **Fit-free, from the mesh's second-order structure.** For adjacent facets
279	     on a cylinder of radius R spanning dihedral θ with width h = Rθ:
280	     \`sag ≈ (h/2)²/(2R) = h²/(8R) = h·θ/8\`. So **sag ≈ dihedral × facet size /
281	     8**, per facet, no fits and no jet inversion — computable in stage 2.1.
282	  3. **Cross-check.** (1) and (2) should agree. Disagreement means the
283	     provenance premise is violated — a decimated, repaired or remeshed STL —
284	     which is itself worth detecting and reporting.
285	
286	  *Prove it before trusting it:* the corpus has reference STEPs and stage 1
287	  \`--compare\` reports the true centroid max distance, so run the reference-free
288	  estimator across the corpus and check it reproduces those numbers. The
289	  reference is the **validation instrument, never the production input**.
290	
291	  *The angular tolerance falls out of the same reasoning.* Its own doc comment
292	  admits it is a stand-in ("Medium-resolution CAD exports step small-radius
293	  fillets up to ~24° between rows… the default must admit those"), and Onshape
294	  exposes the setting directly (12.5° / 6.25° / 2.5° for coarse/medium/fine).
295	  Recover it as a **clustering** rather than a threshold: the dihedral
296	  distribution should be bimodal — tessellation steps near zero, real sharp
297	  edges well separated — so find the gap (Otsu-style). Plot it before
298	  believing it; on stepped_block_toroidal fine only 384 of 51603 edges are called sharp,
299	  so the populations are very lopsided. **Bonus:** the absence of a clean gap,
300	  or a spiky constant-dihedral distribution, is exactly the OpenSCAD
301	  faceted-by-construction detector described in the item below.
302	
303	  *Risks to measure first:*
304	  - ε is not truly global — exporters also cap angular deviation and minimum
305	    facet width, so different features are bound by different constraints. Take
306	    a high quantile or max, **never a median**, or legitimate faces get
307	    rejected.
308	  - Tightening 0.4 → ~1e-3 is a 400× change that moves behaviour everywhere.
309	    \`REFIT_GIVE_UP\` is defined as 8×stol; merge's centroid gate and every
310	    \`face_validates\` use it. Do the sweep harness (item 6 below) first.
311	  - Coarse meshes genuinely have larger sag — the corpus's coarse variants are
312	    the real test, not the fine ones.

321	  The assumption says face-level assignment is the right granularity, which is
322	  exactly the setting for Lloyd-style iteration — this is **Variational Shape
323	  Approximation** (Cohen-Steiner et al.): alternate (assign faces to proxies)
324	  and (refit proxies) to a fixpoint. Half of it already exists and is known to
325	  work here: \`relabel_boundaries\` + \`refit_geometry\` converged this session and
326	  took stepped_block_toroidal fine from 73 surfaces to exactly the reference's 71.

330	    \`relabel_boundaries\`, \`merge_regions\`, \`resplit_disconnected\`.
331	  - **Needs:** an error metric (vertex distance, self-calibrated as in
332	    \`acceptance_tol\`); a proxy-count rule (add a proxy where regional error is

352	  3. Full suite (\`cargo test --release --no-fail-fast\`, ~2.5 min) — decisive.
353	  4. Inert on both ⇒ delete it, and record in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN what it was for
354	     and what superseded it, so the knowledge is not lost with the code.
355	
356	  Re-test in roughly this order, most-likely-dead first:
357	  - \`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`, \`MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO\` — degeneracy guards that
358	    exist because competition builds wrong supports; correct supports should
359	    make them unnecessary.
360	  - \`LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX\` — its rationale is that an analytic surface islanded in
361	    a jagged per-facet blob makes invalid wires; if boundaries are clean chains
362	    after A, that rationale may simply evaporate.
363	  - \`GROW_VTOL_FACTOR\` + \`RESIDUAL_SIGMAS\` — both belong to flood, which C
364	    replaces.
365	  - The whole \`PATCH_*\` / \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\` / \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\` family —
366	    gone with seeds.
367	  - \`MERGE_MAX_SMALL\`, \`SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES\` and the freeform island
368	    rules (\`enclosed\`, \`islands_split_a_patch\`).
369	  - \`MIN_CYLINDER_FACES\` / \`MIN_SPHERE_FACES\` / \`MIN_TORUS_FACES\`.
370	  - Finally, ask whether the **jet** is still needed at all beyond the
371	    ladder's rung ordering (\`region_is_developable\`). It was demoted to a prior
372	    in the 2026-07-20 pivot; C may remove its last real job.
373	
374	  Track the constant count as a headline number: stage 2 carried **35** at the
375	  time of writing (\`grep -h "^const \|^pub(crate) const \|^pub(super) const "
376	  src/stage2/*.rs | wc -l\`). Report it in the commit each time it moves.
377	
378	  *Deliberately not recommended:* seam **detection** proper (finding tangent
379	  seams directly). Curvature-based detection was measured dead in the
380	  2026-07-20 pivot — no threshold separates real junctions from real curvature
381	  gradients, the classes overlap. Structural signals (grid-direction
382	  discontinuity, density change) were never tried but are provenance-dependent
383	  and near-absent on OCCT's Delaunay interiors, so a detector would have to
384	  fall back to competition anyway and would not retire any parameter.
385	
386	- **The NURBS corpus: 5 of 8 models pass, 3 fail, and the failures were
387	  invisible.** Surveyed 2026-07-28 against the reference STEPs that were
388	  already in the repo. **\`bspline_hill\`, \`bspline_terrain\`, \`bspline_wave\`,
389	  \`elliptical_arch\`, \`elliptical_cylinder\` all pass full \`--compare\` at
390	  0.000000e0 mm.** Failing: **\`hill_with_hole\`** (6.73 mm at stage 3.1),
391	  **\`bspline_blister\`** (8.04 mm), **\`bspline_saddle\`** (1.41 mm).
392	
393	  **The correlation is the finding: every NURBS model with a test past stage 1
394	  passes, and every one that fails had stage-1-only coverage.** Five of the
395	  eight were untested past stage 1. These are not hard cases that resisted
396	  effort, they are unwatched ones — and every one has a reference STEP sitting
397	  next to it, so the tests could have been written at any time. Two of them
398	  (\`blister\`, \`saddle\`) had silently collapsed to all-per-facet polyhedra;
399	  that half is now pinned. **Adding \`--compare\` coverage for the whole NURBS
400	  corpus is the first thing to do here, before any fix.**
401	
402	  Two distinct bugs behind the three failures:
403	
404	  **(a) \`hill_with_hole\` — competition carves the freeform hill into 40
405	  spurious analytic patches** (47 surfaces against a reference 7: 5 planes +
406	  1 B-spline + 1 cylinder). The correct 7 are all present and correct — the
407	  hole cylinder is r=5.0000 fitted to 1.8e-6 — with 40 cones/spheres of 4–23
408	  faces and 2–8 mm² area littered over the 1541 mm² hill. \`bspline_hill\` is
409	  the *same hill without the hole* and decomposes perfectly, so the pair is a
410	  controlled reproducer; the difference is that hill_with_hole's mesh is
411	  coarser (1740 faces for the hill vs 3200), and analytic surfaces fit
412	  freeform patches happily at coarse density.
413	
414	  The mechanism that should catch this is the stage-2.3(e) island absorption,
415	  whose candidacy rule (\`SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES = 16\` plus enclosure) is
416	  too weak two ways: the accidents run to 23 faces, and because the same test
417	  decides which surfaces count as "real" for enclosure, the accidents just over
418	  the cap mark *themselves* real and disqualify their smaller neighbours from
419	  absorption too — so a whole cluster survives.
420	
421	  **Four candidacy formulations were tried. Three are measured wrong — do not
422	  re-derive them:**
423	  - **Fit quality** (\`error_max > --vertex-tolerance\`). Tempting, because on
424	    hill_with_hole the accidents sit 200–800× worse than the genuine surfaces
425	    (4.0e-4…1.4e-3 against 1.8e-6), with vertex tolerance in the gap. **Wrong
426	    in general: an accidental fit is frequently an *excellent* fit** — a run of
427	    coplanar facets along an inflection really is planar, a band of a singly
428	    curved patch really is cylindrical. \`bspline_hill\`: 6 surfaces → **2719**.
429	  - **A fraction of the region.** Fixed both hills; broke \`elliptical_arch\`
430	    and \`elliptical_cylinder\`, because a fraction is *stricter* than a count on
431	    small regions and looser on large ones.
432	  - **Smaller than the region's total leftover count.** Fixed hill_with_hole
433	    (exactly 7 surfaces) and kept the ellipticals; broke \`stepped_block\`
434	    medium, 81 → 84 surfaces with a spurious 19th torus — scattered slivers
435	    along real seams are not a freeform patch.
436	  - **Smaller than the largest *connected* leftover patch.** Probe clean, all
437	    eight NURBS models at their best; but it dissolves \`cone_tangent_planes\`'
438	    real 16-face cones into a B-spline and re-breaks the test just re-enabled.
439	
440	  **Why no such rule works, and what it actually needs.** A B-spline can fit
441	  anything, a cone included — so "the freeform patch validates over these
442	  faces" is *not* evidence that the region is freeform, and the absorption
443	  guard has no way to tell a real analytic surface adrift in leftovers from an
444	  accident. The window the corpus demands is 13.5%–18.6% of the patch, which is
445	  overfitting, not a criterion. **This needs genuine model selection between a
446	  B-spline and an analytic surface on the same support.** Note the relation to
447	  item 2: complexity pricing was refuted there for *nested analytic* types,
448	  where the DOF difference is 1–2; here it is a B-spline's dozens against a
449	  cone's six, so the argument that killed it there does not obviously apply and
450	  it is worth re-deriving for this case specifically.
451	
452	  **Also learned: \`cone_tangent_planes\` has been passing for an accidental
453	  reason.** Its region's freeform fit fails as ill-conditioned, and that is the
454	  only thing leaving its cones alone. Give the freeform rung a
455	  better-conditioned domain and it swallows a real cone. Treat that test as
456	  fragile.
457	
458	  **And a second, downstream problem on hill_with_hole**: with the decomposition
459	  corrected to exactly 7 surfaces it clears stage 3.1 and then fails stage 4.1
460	  on **volume, 6.96e-2 relative**. So candidacy is necessary but not sufficient
461	  there — expect a freeform-accuracy or export issue behind it.
462	
463	  **(b) \`bspline_blister\` and \`bspline_saddle\` — the freeform fit itself
464	  fails.** Their large components are rejected by the §2.7 pole-bound check
465	  (blister: one 926-face component, "control points 2749.5 mm outside the data
466	  bounding box"; saddle: three components). The check is doing its job — the
467	  fit really is ill-conditioned — but nothing recovers, so the region falls to
468	  per-facet. This is where to start: it is a fitting problem with a clear
469	  signal, not a model-selection argument.
470	
471	- **REWRITE stage 2.3(b) seeding: kill the blind patch. — NEXT MAJOR WORK on
472	  stage 2.** Design and all supporting measurements are in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN,
473	  "Rewriting stage 2.3(b): the blind patch is the root cause". Read it first;
474	  it records four measured dead ends that must not be re-derived.
475	
476	  **Why.** \`grow_from\` derives its initial fit from an *unvalidated* 32-face BFS
477	  neighbourhood. The patch itself is discarded (the committed face set is always
478	  a \`flood\` output, so every member is validated) — but its contamination lives
479	  on in the fit *parameters*, which produces both observed failures: 323 seeds
480	  returning NO GROWTH, and plausible-but-wrong fits whose members all genuinely
481	  validate. Validation is sound; what it validates against is wrong.
482	
483	  **Step 0 DONE 2026-07-28 — leftover-component ladder rescue (2.3(d)).**
484	  \`rescue_leftover_components\` (classify.rs) runs the whole-region DOF ladder on
485	  each connected component of competition's leftovers, before freeform. No new
486	  math: the existing conical fitter already estimates its axis from the normal
487	  covariance (\`smallest_eigenvector_3x3\`), and given an *uncontaminated* face
488	  set it recovers r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 against true 1.0 / 1.0 / 0.5.
489	  Competition was simply never handing it a clean face set.
490	  Result on \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine: **138 → 108 surfaces**, cylinders
491	  34 → 36 (reference 37), tori 21 → 19 (18), B-splines 3 → 1, per-facet planes
492	  80 → 52. Twelve of fourteen probe models bit-identical; suite 508/0/6.
493	  It can only add surfaces — it runs on faces nothing else explained and every
494	  rung is vertex-tolerance validated.
495	
496	  **Step 1 DONE 2026-07-28 — visited ≠ claimed.** Committing a hypothesis no
497	  longer retires the faces it grew into; every region face seeds exactly once.
498	  \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine **108 → 95 surfaces** (planes 52 → 39), other
499	  13 probe models bit-identical, suite 508/0/6.
500	  **Cost, and it is real: that model goes 42 s → 103 s (~2.5×).** Reseeding
501	  every face unbounded was far worse (>10 min, abandoned), so faces in the
502	  *interior* of the explained set — every neighbour claimed — are skipped:
503	  a fit grown from the middle of a validated surface only refits that surface,
504	  so it is pure cost. Faces at the margin still seed, which is where a
505	  competitor can actually arise. If the runtime becomes a problem, that
506	  interior test is the knob, not the principle.
507	
508	  **Build order for the rest, each step measurable on its own:**
509	  1. ~~**Visited ≠ claimed.**~~ DONE, see above. A seed that commits nothing costs one entry from the
510	     untried list; the faces it touched stay seedable. Makes seed misordering
511	     cost only time (as the plan has always claimed but never delivered) and
512	     makes an unreliable bootstrap survivable — a 36-face surface gets 36
513	     attempts. Small, self-contained, and testable alone.
514	  2. **Identifiability as the commit gate, never residual.** Measured: the three
515	     spurious tori fit at 0.52/0.72/0.87 quanta against genuine surfaces at mean
516	     0.39, max 0.56 — overlapping, because a 7-DOF torus on 7 faces is exactly
517	     determined. Use sweep/conditioning; keep the quantum as the *unit* for
518	     already-pinned fits.
519	  3. ~~**Junction-constrained expansion**~~ **DONE 2026-07-28.**
520	     \`grow_with_axis\` (compete.rs) + \`rescue_with_neighbour_axes\` (2.3(d2),
521	     classify.rs): grow a cylinder whose axis DIRECTION is borrowed from an
522	     adjacent committed surface, largest validated area first, iterating until
523	     nothing more grows. With the axis fixed the fit is a 3-DOF circle in the
524	     perpendicular plane — well conditioned on a short arc — so growth starts
525	     from the seed facet alone and there is no blind patch to straddle.
526	     \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine **95 → 75 surfaces**: planes 39 → 18
527	     (reference 16), cylinders 36 → 38 (37), **tori 19 → 18, exactly the
528	     reference**, B-splines 1. Other 13 probe models bit-identical, suite
529	     508/0/6.
530	     Was: **Junction-constrained expansion** from committed neighbours (\`locus.rs\`).
531	     Measured on the three cylinders this model loses: fixing the axis alone
532	     recovers r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 at 5–10× under vertex tolerance.
533	     The free fit fails on the axis, not the radius.
534	  4. ~~**Fixpoint over seed passes**~~ **DONE 2026-07-28** as a (d)/(d2)
535	     fixpoint — they alternate until neither commits. **Measured inert**: both
536	     already converge in one round on every corpus model. Kept as a guarantee.
537	  5. **Delete \`seed_patch\`/\`condition_patch\` and the \`PATCH_*\` family —
538	     MEASURED NOT YET POSSIBLE, and it is NOT a cleanup.** Cutting the blind
539	     patch to the seed alone (\`SEED_PATCH_CAP\` = 1) collapses
540	     \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine to **24872 surfaces**, one per facet. The
541	     blind patch is still the only source of initial parameters for the FREE
542	     fit path, which commits ~63 hypotheses before either rescue runs; (d) and
543	     (d2) merely clean up after it. **The prerequisite is a bootstrap for the
544	     free path that is not a blind neighbourhood.** That is a design step, not
545	     a deletion. Constant count stays at 34.
546	
547	     **Breadcrumbs for a viable seeding approach — everything here is measured
548	     this session, so start from it rather than re-deriving:**
549	     - **The bootstrap does NOT need to be reliable. It needs to be cheap and
550	       DOF-reducing.** Two facts combine to make that true. First, sagitta
551	       arithmetic says no local patch can determine a *free* surface of
552	       revolution by any method (over a 7.5° arc on r = 0.5 the sagitta is
553	       ~0.001 mm against a 2e-5 mm quantum → r ~2% wrong = 200× vtol), so
554	       improving the *fit* is hopeless; only removing DOF helps. Second,
555	       **visited ≠ claimed (already built) makes a failed seed cost only its own
556	       turn**, so a 36-face surface gets 36 independent attempts. An
557	       unreliable-but-cheap bootstrap is therefore survivable in a way it was
558	       not before.
559	     - **The failure is the AXIS, not the radius** — measured. Fix the axis and
560	       the fit is a 3-DOF circle in the perpendicular plane
561	       (\`fit_circle_for_axis\`, cylindrical.rs), well conditioned even on a short
562	       arc: r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 from arcs of 65°/60°/40°.
563	     - **So: per seed, try a small set of CANDIDATE AXES, each giving a
564	       constrained fit from the seed facet alone, and let largest-validated-area
565	       choose.** No neighbourhood is ever fitted blind. Candidate sources, best
566	       first:
567	       1. An adjacent committed surface's axis — implemented as 2.3(d2).
568	          **DO NOT simply generalise it from "leftovers only" to "every seed" —
569	          measured wrong 2026-07-28, twice.** Offering \`grow_with_axis\` as a
570	          per-seed competitor took \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine from **75 to
571	          100 surfaces, cylinders fragmenting 38 → 63**; restricting it to seeds
572	          where nothing free grew gave the *same* 100. **Why it works in (d2)
573	          and not per-seed: (d2) selects the LARGEST validated area over the
574	          whole leftover set before committing anything, whereas a per-seed
575	          candidate commits whatever grows locally.** A cylinder on a borrowed
576	          axis is frequently a locally-valid sub-piece of the real one — it
577	          validates at vertex tolerance over 20–30 faces and wins its own local
578	          contest, then fragments the surface it belonged to. Any use of a
579	          borrowed axis outside (d2) needs to keep that global selection, not
580	          just the constrained fit.
581	       2. The jet's principal direction at the seed (the κ ≈ 0 direction of a
582	          developable). Free, per-face, no patch. Accurate where tessellation is
583	          uniform — four of six sampled fillet strips read κ₁ = 2.005 against a
584	          true 2.0 — and wrong where a face's vertex neighbourhood spans a
585	          junction, which is exactly what cheap failures absorb.
586	       3. Normal covariance (\`smallest_eigenvector_3x3\`, already in conical.rs)
587	          over the seed's immediate ring.
588	       4. The seed facet's long-edge direction. **Corroborating only** — right
589	          ~65% of the time on strips, with adjacent strips differing by up to
590	          76°. Never load-bearing.
591	     - **Planes need no bootstrap at all**: one facet determines a plane
592	       exactly, so the planar candidate can fit the seed alone and flood.
593	     - **For spheres** the centre lies along the seed facet's normal at distance
594	       r, so the jet's κ gives a 1-DOF start. **Tori are the open case** — 7 DOF
595	       with no equally cheap constraint; the bent-tube promotion in 2.3(c) is
596	       the existing partial answer.
597	     - **Validate against \`stepped_block_toroidal\` medium and
598	       \`part_rounded_cube\` coarse**, not just fine: the coarse-mesh curved
599	       bootstrap is where the jet is genuinely unreliable (κ₁ = 0.023 / 0.162 /
600	       2.2 across one exact r = 0.5 arc) and is the one place the blind patch is
601	       currently irreplaceable.
602	
603	  **The one real risk**, and it is narrower than "no anchor" (seeding stays
604	  universal, so with nothing committed the design degrades to today's behaviour,
605	  which already finds 34/37 cylinders and 18/18 tori): the **coarse-mesh curved
606	  bootstrap**. A plane is exact from one facet; on fine meshes the jet is good
607	  (4 of 6 sampled strips read 2.005 against a true 2.0); but the κ₁ = 0.023 /
608	  0.162 / 2.2 spread over one r=0.5 arc was measured on stepped_block **medium**.
609	  That is where the blind patch is irreplaceable today. **Validate any
610	  replacement against stepped_block_toroidal medium and part_rounded_cube coarse
611	  before deleting anything.**
612	
613	  **Also re-enables**: the three tests disabled 2026-07-28 (two in
614	  stage2_competition.rs, one in stage4_integration.rs) all carry this as their
615	  blocker.
616	
617	- **stepped_block_toroidal: the seeding is scale-blind — and face size is NOT
618	  the fix.** Diagnosis in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN, "Why stepped_block_toroidal is hard".
619	  This is the model every stage-2 threshold sits on a cliff for; it is one
620	  tangent-connected region of 26122 faces, so all 71 reference surfaces come out
621	  of competition.
622	
623	  **The mechanism is established.** 89% of faces are fillet facets covering
624	  224 mm²; the planes are 2.5% of faces covering 13783 mm². \`seed_patch\` grows
625	  a depth-3 BFS neighbourhood capped at \`SEED_PATCH_CAP = 32\` from surfaces that
626	  often have fewer than 32 faces (a 3232 mm² plane is **12 faces**; 72 of 75
627	  connected flat components are under 32). Measured: of 200 sampled flat seeds,
628	  **zero** produced a patch that stayed on the flat — median 50% fillet, worst
629	  96%. \`PATCH_RADII\` is the one spatial bound and is explicitly disabled for
630	  flats. So the constants are face counts standing in for lengths, which is why
631	  every sweep of them is non-monotonic.
632	
633	  **MEASURED DEAD END — do not implement a face-size test.** The obvious fix
634	  ("a facet much smaller than its neighbour is across a boundary") is not merely
635	  unreliable, it is **anti-correlated**. Neighbour size ratio (√area):
636	  *across* a flat|curved junction the median is **1.26** (p90 15); *within* the
637	  flats it is p75 **18.7**, p90 **47.5**. The discontinuity is inside the
638	  planes, not at their edges — a 4380 mm² plane spans 0.048…54.36 mm (1100×
639	  within one surface): one huge interior quad ringed by fine slivers, and of the
640	  170 faces touching a fillet the median size is 0.118 mm, *finer* than the
641	  0.52 mm fillet facets they abut. This is constrained-Delaunay grading, exactly
642	  as predicted by **Mesh provenance** — the plane's triangulation must match the
643	  fillet's edge discretization along the shared edge. A size-ratio detector
644	  would fire inside planes and stay silent at junctions. The weaker version
645	  (keep the seed patch *scale-homogeneous*) dies with it: a 0.118 mm plane
646	  sliver and a 0.52 mm fillet facet are "comparable".
647	
648	  **Partially explored, unresolved:** classify by *exact face normals* instead
649	  of the jet. Motivation: the jet is unreliable here (adjacent jet-"flat" faces
650	  have a median dihedral of 1.24°, so the jet's flat set is not the planes),
651	  while facet normals are exact and \`curved|curved\` dihedrals are a tight 2.44°
652	  (p90 2.51) against the exporter's angular tolerance. But connected components
653	  over near-zero-dihedral edges give **342 components above 10 mm² where the
654	  reference has 16 planes**, and the count is insensitive to the threshold from
655	  0.001° to 0.2°. The top ~16 sum to 14301 mm² against an expected 13783, so the
656	  planes are in there with a 326-component tail that is not yet explained.
657	  **Next step: identify that tail.** Classify mesh faces against the reference
658	  STEP's 16 planes to get ground truth, then ask what the extra components are —
659	  large-radius fillets whose dihedral steps are below the threshold
660	  (\`curved|curved\` p10 is 0.70°, so some curved edges are much flatter than
661	  2.5°) is the leading hypothesis. If it is, the separation is between "constant
662	  dihedral" and "zero dihedral", not between flat and curved, and wants a
663	  different statistic.
664	
665	  Diagnostic: \`dump_regions <model> jetdump\` prints per-face k1/k2/area,
666	  adjacency and normal — all the measurements above came from it.
667	
668	- **Faceted-by-construction sources (OpenSCAD, Tinkercad, Blender): a flag or
669	  auto-detection.** Not a bug today, and not being worried about yet — recorded
670	  so the eventual decision is informed. These have no B-rep at any stage, so
671	  there are no tangent junctions to recover, only discretization steps, and the
672	  seam assumption that carries the whole pipeline holds trivially but conveys
673	  no information (every edge is a facet boundary). Full findings, with the
674	  fragment-count arithmetic and the thresholds, are under **Mesh provenance and
675	  the seam assumption** in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md. The headlines:
676	  - \`--angular-tolerance\` (default 25°) silently decides whether an OpenSCAD
677	    revolution comes out as an analytic surface or as the prism it literally
678	    is: \`$fn ≥ 15\` reads smooth, \`$fn ≤ 14\` reads sharp. On OpenSCAD's own
679	    defaults that is radius ≳ 4.5 mm. A \`$fn = 6\` hex nut is handled correctly
680	    by accident of the same rule.
681	  - The ambiguity is *genuine* — a 15-gon prism and a coarse cylinder are the
682	    same mesh — so this is a policy choice, not something to be measured. That
683	    argues for an explicit flag over silent auto-detection, or at least for
684	    auto-detection that reports what it decided.
685	  - Expected failure mode where a fit *is* attempted: large radii at default
686	    resolution, where the sagitta \`r(1 − cos(180/n))\` breaks
687	    \`--surface-tolerance\` (0.55 mm at r = 100, n = 30) and the surface falls to
688	    per-facet planes. Vertex validation never fails, because OpenSCAD inscribes
689	    its polygons and the vertices sit exactly on the ideal surface.
690	  - Detection signals if we go that way: a constant dihedral of exactly 360/n
691	    all the way around a revolution (B-rep tessellation steps vary, and density
692	    changes at seams), and vertices exactly on the circumscribed circle.
693	  - Worth a corpus model or two before deciding anything.
694	
695	- **Make the stage-2 decomposition robust rather than tuned.** Stage 2 carries
696	  ~25 behavioural thresholds, and several sit one step from catastrophe: on
697	  \`stepped_block_toroidal\` medium, four *unrelated* knobs — \`GROW_VTOL_FACTOR\`
698	  ×3.3, \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\` 6→4, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\` 16→32, and capping the
699	  fallback seed patch — each flip the model from 83 surfaces to a full collapse
700	  to per-facet planes. When unrelated parameters all sit on a cliff the values
701	  are not the problem, the structure is. (This is the same failure mode the
702	  2026-07-17 pivot was called for; it re-grew on the new architecture within a
703	  fortnight, so it is worth watching for deliberately.) Ranked candidates, each
704	  aimed at *replacing* guards rather than adding one:
705	  0. **Self-calibrating acceptance tolerance — REMOVED 2026-07-28.** Deleted
706	     outright (\`acceptance_tol\`, \`residual_scale\`, \`GROW_VTOL_FACTOR\`,
707	     \`RESIDUAL_SIGMAS\`); the flood bar is \`--vertex-tolerance\`. Stage-2
708	     constant count 36 → 34. Full write-up in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN, "The flood bar
709	     is the vertex tolerance". Headlines:
710	     - **It was masking a seed-conditioning defect, not doing its stated job.**
711	       Its response is non-monotonic (stepped_block_toroidal fine: 138 / 90 / 134 / 113
712	       / 71 surfaces at 1× / 2× / 5× / 12× / 30×), which is a lever, not a
713	       bound. And \`residual_scale\` measured interpolation error over the fit's
714	       own support and spent it on extrapolation to faces outside it.
715	     - **The 2026-07-21 evidence for it was stale**: "everything returned NO
716	       GROWTH" without it is no longer true — 12 of 14 probe models are
717	       bit-identical at a bar of exactly \`--vertex-tolerance\`.
718	     - **Won**: \`bspline_blister\` 968 → 7 surfaces (reference 6),
719	       \`hill_with_hole\` 47 → 8 (reference 7). The spurious analytic patches
720	       were what had prevented their freeform fits from taking the region.
721	     - **Cost, now blocking 3 disabled tests**: stepped_block_toroidal fine 71 → 138,
722	       via 323 no-growth seeds — including flat seeds failing to grow a plane.

745	     surface types nest, so a richer model can always match a simpler one's
746	     coverage and add a little more — and that an MDL-style score charging for
747	     parameters would subsume all five. Two measurements killed it.
748	     *First, the arithmetic.* Coverage pricing cannot reach the case it was
749	     aimed at. The degenerate torus on \`stepped_block_toroidal\` covers 180
750	     faces where the cylinder it displaces covers 94: 86 extra faces for 2
751	     extra degrees of freedom. Any price that rejects it must charge more than
752	     ~43 faces per DOF, which would also forbid a cylinder from ever beating a
753	     plane by fewer than 86 faces — wrong everywhere else. The degenerate fit
754	     genuinely *is* the better description of its own support; MDL is right and
755	     unhelpful. The fault is not in the scoring, it is that the support is
756	     stolen from a neighbour, which greedy per-seed selection cannot see.
757	     *Second, the guards had already stopped being five.* Ablated individually
758	     against per-model surface-type breakdowns (\`tmp/probe.sh\`, 14 models) plus
759	     the full suite: \`radius_plausible\` is **completely inert** — deleted, along

779	     real; the two obvious answers are both rejected. Open, unassigned.*

791	       effort is trying to remove, and it silently destroys the case where a
792	       part legitimately has two nearly-equal radii.

802	       2.9996/3.0004 as evidence that those two supports still disagree about

813	       explain a minority of the region". Both were tried.
```

```
1	//! Stage 2.3(b): scoped fit competition.
2	//!
3	//! For a smooth region the whole-region ladder (2.3(a)) cannot explain — a
4	//! tangent chain like the rounded-cube shell (6 planes, 12 fillet cylinders,
5	//! 8 corner spheres) or the nosecone's cone+sphere — decompose it by
6	//! seed-first multi-type competition run INSIDE the region. The sharp
7	//! boundary cordons the arena, so competition cost is bounded and
8	//! cross-region accidents are impossible.
9	//!
10	//! - **Seeds**: every region face seeds exactly once, in jet-interior-first
11	//!   order (faces whose (κ1,κ2) best match their neighbors' seed before
12	//!   junction rows). Committing a hypothesis does NOT retire the faces it grew
13	//!   into — growing into a face is a hypothesis, not a conclusion, and a face
14	//!   swallowed by a wrong surface must still be able to seed the right one
15	//!   (\`resolve_claims\` arbitrates the resulting multi-claims). Only faces in
16	//!   the *interior* of the explained set are skipped, since a fit grown from
17	//!   the middle of a validated surface merely refits that surface.
18	//! - **Per seed**: grow each candidate surface type (jet-pruned) from a
19	//!   small jet-similar seed patch — fit, flood-accept every region face the
20	//!   fit validates, refit on the grown support, to a fixpoint — and commit
21	//!   the hypothesis with the **largest validated area** (simplest-DOF
22	//!   tiebreak). The jet only orders and prunes; vertex-tolerance validation
23	//!   decides every face.
24	//! - **Claims**: a flood may grow through faces already claimed by an
25	//!   earlier hypothesis (junction rows that genuinely lie on two surfaces),
26	//!   so a face can be claimed by several hypotheses. \`resolve_claims\`
27	//!   assigns each contested face to the claimant that fits its vertices best
28	//!   (2× margin to leave the larger claimant).
29	//! - **Toroidal candidates (stage 2.3(c))**: a torus competes like any other
30	//!   type — grown directly from curved seeds (fillet tori, bend tubes), and
31	//!   additionally *promoted* from a cylinder whose flood stalled against
32	//!   faces whose jet continues the tube (κ1 ≈ the tube's 1/r, |κ2| well
33	//!   below κ1 — a bent tube's signature). The promotion seeds its fit from
34	//!   the pure-bend patch just past the stall line: the cylinder fit itself
35	//!   locates the tangency, so the patch cannot straddle it the way a
36	//!   jet-class patch can (bend and straight tube share κ1).
37	//!
38	//! **Soundness** (the sub-region lemma, under exactness): an accidental fit
39	//! is supported on a *sub-region*; the true surface fitted from an interior
40	//! seed floods — vertex tolerance the arbiter — to its entire true extent,
41	//! and largest-validated-area then selects it over the accidental one.
42	//!
43	//! Faces no hypothesis claims are returned as leftovers; the caller degrades
44	//! them to per-facet planes (freeform fitting is stage 2.3(e), a later step).
45	
46	use std::collections::HashSet;
47	
48	use crate::config::Config;
49	use crate::stage1::ConnectedMesh;
50	
51	use super::surface::SurfaceType;

53	use super::edges::JetField;
54	use super::planar::PlanarSurface;
55	use super::regions::Region;
56	use super::spherical::{bounding_box_diagonal, SphericalSurface};
57	use super::toroidal::ToroidalSurface;

59	    face_area, ConicalHypothesis, PlanarHypothesis, SphericalHypothesis, ToroidalHypothesis,
60	    MIN_CYLINDER_FACES, MIN_SPHERE_FACES, MIN_TORUS_FACES,
61	};

63	/// Max faces in the jet-similar seed patch used to condition the initial fit.
64	const SEED_PATCH_CAP: usize = 32;
65	
66	/// Max trim rounds when conditioning a seed patch (one worst face per round).
67	const PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS: usize = 16;
68	
69	/// Floor on the patch size while conditioning — below this an analytic fit is
70	/// under-determined and trimming would chase noise.
71	const MIN_CONDITION_FACES: usize = 6;
72	
73	/// Max BFS depth of the seed patch. Kept shallow so the patch stays local to
74	/// the seed and does not bleed across a tangent junction between two surfaces
75	/// of the same curvature class (e.g. a cone meeting a cylinder, where the
76	/// curvature is continuous across the seam) — a straddling patch fits neither
77	/// surface. An interior seed's shallow neighbourhood is on one surface, which
78	/// is all the initial fit needs; the flood then grows it to the true extent.
79	const MAX_PATCH_DEPTH: usize = 3;
80	
81	/// Seed-patch radius bound, measured in radii of curvature of the seed's own
82	/// jet. \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\` bounds the patch in *hops*, which stops bounding it
83	/// in space once the facets are large: on a fillet network swept coarsely along
84	/// its own length, three hops walk many millimetres around the fillet chain and
85	/// straddle several of its segments. A surface of radius r is only locally
86	/// itself over a few r, and a few r of support is all any fit needs — the flood
87	/// grows the rest. Flats (jet magnitude ~0) are unbounded, as they should be.
88	const PATCH_RADII: f64 = 4.0;
89	
90	/// Refit-and-reflood rounds while growing one hypothesis.
91	const MAX_GROW_ROUNDS: usize = 8;
92	
93	// \`GROW_VTOL_FACTOR\` (30× vertex tolerance) and \`RESIDUAL_SIGMAS\` (5) lived
94	// here, together with \`acceptance_tol\`/\`residual_scale\`. The flood bar is now
95	// \`--vertex-tolerance\` itself. See DEVELOPMENT_PLAN, "The flood bar is the
96	// vertex tolerance".
97	
98	/// Max BFS depth of the pure-bend fit patch grown past a stalled cylinder's
99	/// frontier (bent-tube promotion). A few rows of bend arc condition the
100	/// 7-DOF torus fit; the flood then claims the rest.
101	const BEND_PATCH_DEPTH: usize = 4;
102	
103	/// Face cap of the pure-bend fit patch.
104	const BEND_PATCH_CAP: usize = 64;
105	
106	/// How far a surface of revolution's support must sweep around its own axis, in
107	/// degrees, before the radius counts as determined by the data.
108	///
109	/// This is an *identifiability* test, and worth naming as such because the
110	/// distinction is easy to lose: it does not ask which surface fits best, it
111	/// asks whether the data pins the parameter at all. A fit whose parameter is
112	/// unpinned is not a slightly-worse explanation, it is a free variable that
113	/// happened to land somewhere, and no amount of comparing residuals or coverage
114	/// between candidates can detect it — see the note on complexity pricing in
115	/// TODO.md for why the obvious model-selection fix does not work here.
116	///
117	/// A patch subtending a negligible angle about the axis never observes the
118	/// revolution, so the radius is free — and at the limit of a free radius each
119	/// type collapses into the simpler one below it: a torus with R → ∞ is a
120	/// cylinder, a cylinder with r → ∞ is a plane. Such a fit is not merely
121	/// useless, it actively wins. Being a super-model of the simpler surface it
122	/// covers everything that surface covers, then floods a little further on the
123	/// slack — over the first tessellation row past a tangent junction, whose
124	/// chordal sag the simpler surface cannot take — and so carries the
125	/// largest-validated-area contest every time.
126	///
127	/// Measured on \`stepped_block_toroidal_fillets\` fine, where neither population
128	/// overlaps by anything close to this bar:
129	///
130	/// - **Tori.** The 18 real corner blends sweep 84.7°–103.4° and fit R to the
131	///   reference's exact 2.0000 / 2.5000 / 14.0000. Six impostors astride the
132	///   r = 0.5 and r = 1 perimeter fillets sweep 3.1°–6.7° and report
133	///   R = 20, 26, 29, 37, 87, 114 — junk, each an unconstrained parameter's
134	///   arbitrary landing place, and every one of them inside the model's own
135	///   121 mm bounding box, which is why bounding the radius never caught them.
136	/// - **Cylinders.** Every real one sweeps ≥ 64.8°. Four impostors sweep
137	///   2.8°–2.9°, each a flat wall plus the first tessellation row of the four
138	///   fillets bordering it (those rows sit 2.9e-3 mm off the wall plane, over
139	///   the flood tolerance, so the plane that should have won them cannot).
140	///
141	/// 20° sits 3× above every impostor and 3× below every real surface. Note that
142	/// sagitta-versus-tolerance does *not* separate them — the toroidal impostors'
143	/// spines still deviate 0.016–0.042 mm, well above the flood tolerance. The
144	/// angle is what matters, not the sag.
145	///
146	/// This is the counterpart to \`MIN_HALF_ANGLE_DEG\` in conical.rs, which rejects
147	/// a cone too shallow to be told apart from a cylinder.
148	const MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG: f64 = 20.0;
149	
150	/// How many flood-start faces a toroidal candidate tries before giving up
151	/// (each attempt refits from the same initial patch, so extras only matter
152	/// when the best start happens not to validate its own fit).
153	const MAX_TORUS_STARTS: usize = 4;

155	/// A committed analytic hypothesis from competition, tagged by type.
156	pub(super) enum CompeteHyp {
157	    Planar(PlanarHypothesis),
158	    Conical(ConicalHypothesis),
159	    Spherical(SphericalHypothesis),
160	    Toroidal(ToroidalHypothesis),
161	}
162	
163	impl CompeteHyp {
164	    pub(super) fn faces(&self) -> &[usize] {
165	        match self {
166	            CompeteHyp::Planar(h) => &h.faces,
167	            CompeteHyp::Conical(h) => &h.faces,
168	            CompeteHyp::Spherical(h) => &h.faces,
169	            CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h) => &h.faces,
170	        }
171	    }
172	    /// Short surface-type name for diagnostics.
173	    fn kind_name(&self) -> &'static str {
174	        match self {
175	            CompeteHyp::Planar(_) => "plane",
176	            CompeteHyp::Conical(h) => {
177	                if h.is_cylinder() {
178	                    "cyl"
179	                } else {
180	                    "cone"
181	                }
182	            }
183	            CompeteHyp::Spherical(_) => "sphere",
184	            CompeteHyp::Toroidal(_) => "torus",
185	        }
186	    }
187	    /// Simplest-first rank for claim resolution (a face both a cylinder and a
188	    /// torus explain within tolerance is the cylinder's). Matches the ladder's
189	    /// ascending-DOF order.
190	    fn dof_rank(&self) -> u8 {
191	        match self {
192	            CompeteHyp::Planar(_) => 0,
193	            CompeteHyp::Spherical(_) => 1,
194	            CompeteHyp::Conical(_) => 2,
195	            CompeteHyp::Toroidal(_) => 3,
196	        }
197	    }
198	    fn set_faces(&mut self, faces: Vec<usize>, verts: Vec<usize>) {
199	        match self {
200	            CompeteHyp::Planar(h) => {
201	                h.faces = faces;
202	                h.vertices = verts;
203	            }
204	            CompeteHyp::Conical(h) => {
205	                h.faces = faces;
206	                h.vertices = verts;
207	            }
208	            CompeteHyp::Spherical(h) => {
209	                h.faces = faces;
210	                h.vertices = verts;
211	            }
212	            CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h) => {
213	                h.faces = faces;
214	                h.vertices = verts;
215	            }
216	        }
217	    }
218	}
219	
220	/// Decompose a tangent-chain region. Returns the committed analytic
221	/// hypotheses (a strict partition after claim resolution) plus the leftover
222	/// faces no hypothesis explained.
223	pub(super) fn compete_region(
224	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
225	    region: &Region,
226	    jet: &[JetField],
227	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
228	    config: &Config,
229	) -> (Vec<CompeteHyp>, Vec<usize>) {
230	    let n = mesh.faces.len();
231	    let debug = config.verbosity >= 3 || std::env::var("BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE").is_ok();
232	    let mut in_region = vec![false; n];
233	    for &fi in &region.faces {
234	        in_region[fi] = true;
235	    }
236	
237	    // Region curvature scale: a face reads "flat" when its principal
238	    // curvature radius exceeds a couple of region diameters.
239	    let extent = region_extent(mesh, region);
240	    let flat_eps = if extent > 0.0 { 1.0 / (2.0 * extent) } else { 1e-6 };
241	
242	    // Seed order: jet-interior-first (ascending curvature mismatch with
243	    // region neighbors), ties by face index for determinism.
244	    let mut seeds = region.faces.clone();
245	    let score: Vec<f64> = (0..n)
246	        .map(|fi| {
247	            if !in_region[fi] {
248	                return f64::INFINITY;
249	            }
250	            neighbor_jet_mismatch(mesh, fi, jet, &in_region)
251	        })
252	        .collect();
253	    seeds.sort_by(|&a, &b| {
254	        score[a]
255	            .partial_cmp(&score[b])
256	            .unwrap()
257	            .then_with(|| a.cmp(&b))
258	    });
259	
260	    // Every region face seeds exactly once — \`seeds\` is a permutation of them,
261	    // which is what terminates the loop. Committing a hypothesis does NOT
262	    // remove the faces it grew into from the seed pool.
263	    //
264	    // Growing into a face is a hypothesis, not a conclusion. The committed
265	    // face set is always a \`flood\` output, so every member validates — but
266	    // against a fit whose parameters came from an unvalidated seed
267	    // neighbourhood, so "validated" does not mean "the right surface". Letting
268	    // those faces seed too means a wrong hypothesis can be out-competed by a
269	    // right one grown from a face it had swallowed; \`resolve_claims\` already
270	    // exists to arbitrate the multi-claims that produces.
271	    //
272	    // It also makes the long-standing claim that seed misordering "costs only
273	    // time" actually true: previously an early bad hypothesis consumed faces a
274	    // later, better seed needed.
275	    let mut committed: Vec<CompeteHyp> = Vec::new();
276	    let mut claim_lists: Vec<Vec<usize>> = vec![Vec::new(); n]; // face -> committed idxs
277	    let mut claimed = vec![false; n];
278	
279	    for &seed in &seeds {
280	        // Skip only faces in the INTERIOR of what is already explained: every
281	        // neighbour claimed too. Seeding there cannot produce a competitor —
282	        // a fit grown from the middle of a validated surface refits that same
283	        // surface — so it is pure cost. Faces at the margin still seed.
284	        if claimed[seed] && interior_of_claims(mesh, seed, &claimed) {
285	            continue;
286	        }
287	        let jf = jet[seed];
288	        // Dominant / secondary principal-curvature MAGNITUDES. The jet orders
289	        // k1 ≥ k2 by signed value, so for a concave feature (a hole wall,
290	        // κ = −1/r) the dominant curvature is negative and lands in k2 —
291	        // taking |k1| as the dominant would read the wall's flat axial
292	        // direction and treat the hole as planar. Order by magnitude instead.
293	        let a1 = jf.k1.abs().max(jf.k2.abs());
294	        let a2 = jf.k1.abs().min(jf.k2.abs());
295	
296	        // Grow the jet-pruned candidate types; keep the largest validated
297	        // area (simplest-DOF tiebreak).
298	        let mut best: Option<(CompeteHyp, f64, u8)> = None; // (hyp, area, dof)
299	        let consider = |hyp_area: Option<(CompeteHyp, f64)>, dof: u8, best: &mut Option<(CompeteHyp, f64, u8)>| {
300	            if let Some((h, area)) = hyp_area {
301	                let sweep = candidate_sweep(mesh, &h);
302	                if sweep < MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG.to_radians() {
303	                    if debug {
304	                        eprintln!(
305	                            "    compete: seed {seed} rejected {} sweeping only {:.1}\u{00b0} about its axis — radius undetermined ({} faces, area {area:.2})",
306	                            h.kind_name(),
307	                            sweep.to_degrees(),
308	                            h.faces().len(),
309	                        );
310	                    }
311	                    return;
312	                }
313	                let better = match best {
314	                    None => true,
315	                    Some((_, ba, bd)) => area > *ba + 1e-9 || ((area - *ba).abs() <= 1e-9 && dof < *bd),
316	                };
317	                if better {
318	                    *best = Some((h, area, dof));
319	                }
320	            }
321	        };
322	
323	        // Plane is always a candidate (cheap; catches misread flats).
324	        let plane = grow::<PlanarSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, 1);
325	        let mut cone_area = None;
326	        let mut sphere_area = None;
327	        let mut torus_area = None;
328	        if a1 >= flat_eps {
329	            // Curved: a developable (cylinder/cone) is always plausible.
330	            let cone = grow::<ConicalSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, MIN_CYLINDER_FACES);
331	            cone_area = cone.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
332	
333	            // Toroidal candidate: a direct grow from this seed (fillet tori,
334	            // bend tubes), skipped when the seed reads sphere-like — every
335	            // adjacent quad-row band of a sphere lies near some torus, so a
336	            // (κ,κ) seed's torus is at best a bogus band that loses to the
337	            // sphere anyway. A torus seed reads |κ2| < |κ1| (κ2 varies
338	            // azimuthally around the tube; it only approaches κ1 near the
339	            // degenerate R≈r hole of a donut — a whole-region ladder case,
340	            // not a competition case).
341	            let mut torus = if a2 < 0.8 * a1 {
342	                let patch = seed_patch(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet);
343	                torus_candidate(mesh, &in_region, &patch, &[seed], centroids, config)
344	            } else {
345	                None
346	            };
347	            // Bent-tube promotion: a cylinder that stalled against
348	            // tube-continuation faces is the developable prefix of a bent
349	            // tube; grow a torus from the pure-bend patch past the stall.
350	            if let Some((cyl, _, cyl_faces)) = cone.as_ref() {
351	                if cyl.is_cylinder() {
352	                    let promoted =
353	                        promote_bend(mesh, &in_region, cyl, cyl_faces, jet, centroids, config);
354	                    if let Some(p) = promoted {
355	                        if torus.as_ref().is_none_or(|(_, a, _)| p.1 > *a) {
356	                            torus = Some(p);
357	                        }
358	                    }
359	                }
360	            }
361	            torus_area = torus.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
362	
363	            consider(cone.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Conical(h), a)), 2, &mut best);
364	            // Curved in both directions → also try a sphere.
365	            if a2 >= 0.3 * a1 {
366	                let sphere = grow::<SphericalSurface>(mesh, &in_region, seed, jet, centroids, config, MIN_SPHERE_FACES);
367	                sphere_area = sphere.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
368	                consider(sphere.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Spherical(h), a)), 1, &mut best);
369	            }
370	            consider(torus.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h), a)), 3, &mut best);
371	        }
372	        let plane_area = plane.as_ref().map(|(_, a, f)| (*a, f.len()));
373	        consider(plane.map(|(h, a, _)| (CompeteHyp::Planar(h), a)), 0, &mut best);
374	
375	        if debug && best.is_none() {
376	            eprintln!(
377	                "    compete: seed {seed} jet=({:.3},{:.3}) class {:?} → NO GROWTH (plane {plane_area:?} cone {cone_area:?} sphere {sphere_area:?} torus {torus_area:?})",
378	                jf.k1, jf.k2, curvature_class(jf),
379	            );
380	        }
381	
382	        if let Some((hyp, area, dof)) = best {
383	            if debug {
384	                eprintln!(
385	                    "    compete: seed {seed} → {} ({} faces, area {area:.2}, dof {dof}, sweep {:.1}\u{00b0})",
386	                    hyp.kind_name(),
387	                    hyp.faces().len(),
388	                    match &hyp {
389	                        CompeteHyp::Toroidal(t) => torus_sweep(mesh, t).to_degrees(),
390	                        CompeteHyp::Conical(c) => conical_sweep(mesh, c).to_degrees(),
391	                        _ => 0.0,
392	                    }
393	                );
394	            }
395	            let idx = committed.len();
396	            for &f in hyp.faces() {
397	                claim_lists[f].push(idx);
398	                claimed[f] = true;
399	            }
400	            committed.push(hyp);
401	        }
402	        // A seed that grows nothing analytic costs only its own turn.
403	    }
404	
405	    resolve_claims(
406	        mesh,
407	        &region.faces,
408	        &mut committed,
409	        &claim_lists,
410	        config.vertex_tolerance_mm,
411	        config,
412	    )
413	}
414	
415	/// Resolve multi-claimed faces to their best-fitting claimant (2× margin to
416	/// leave the larger claimant), rebuild each hypothesis's face/vertex lists,
417	/// drop hypotheses that lost all faces, and collect unclaimed leftovers.
418	/// Is every neighbour of \`fi\` claimed too? Such a face is interior to the
419	/// explained set and cannot seed anything new.
420	fn interior_of_claims(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, claimed: &[bool]) -> bool {
421	    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
422	    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
423	    face.neighbors[..vc]
424	        .iter()
425	        .all(|&ni| ni < 0 || claimed[ni as usize])
426	}
427	
428	/// Grow a cylinder whose axis DIRECTION is supplied by an already-committed
429	/// neighbour instead of being estimated from an unvalidated seed neighbourhood.
430	///
431	/// This is the point of stage 2.3(d2). A free 5-DOF cylinder fit on a leftover
432	/// arc fails on the **axis**, not the radius: measured on the three fillet
433	/// cylinders \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine loses, fixing only the axis
434	/// direction recovers r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 against true 1.0 / 1.0 /
435	/// 0.5, with residuals five to ten times *under* vertex tolerance. With the
436	/// axis fixed the fit is a 3-DOF circle in the perpendicular plane, which is
437	/// well conditioned on a short arc — so growth can start from the seed facet
438	/// alone and there is no blind patch to straddle a junction.
439	pub(super) fn grow_with_axis(
440	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
```
</details>
