612	/// Grow a hypothesis of type \`S\` from \`seed\`: condition an initial fit on a
613	/// jet-similar seed patch, then flood-accept every region face the fit
614	/// validates and refit on the grown support, to a fixpoint. Returns the
615	/// hypothesis, its validated area, and its face list.
616	fn grow<S: SurfaceType>(
617	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
618	    in_region: &[bool],
619	    seed: usize,
620	    jet: &[JetField],
621	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
622	    config: &Config,
623	    min_faces: usize,
624	) -> Option<(S::Hypothesis, f64, Vec<usize>)> {
625	    let patch = seed_patch(mesh, in_region, seed, jet);
626	    grow_from::<S>(mesh, in_region, seed, patch, centroids, config, min_faces)
627	}
628	
629	/// The grow loop proper, from an explicit initial fit patch and flood start:
630	/// fit the patch, flood-accept every region face the fit validates, refit on
631	/// the grown support, to a fixpoint. Returns the hypothesis, its validated
632	/// area, and its face list.
633	fn grow_from<S: SurfaceType>(
634	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
635	    in_region: &[bool],
636	    start: usize,
637	    initial: Vec<usize>,
638	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
639	    config: &Config,
640	    min_faces: usize,
641	) -> Option<(S::Hypothesis, f64, Vec<usize>)> {
642	    let stol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
643	    let vtol = config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
644	    let mut accepted =
645	        condition_patch::<S>(mesh, start, initial, centroids, config, vtol, stol, min_faces);
646	    let mut prev = 0usize;
647	    for round in 0..MAX_GROW_ROUNDS {
648	        let verts = vertices_of(mesh, &accepted);
649	        let mut fit = S::fit_surface(mesh, &accepted, &verts, config.verbosity)?;
650	        S::determine_convexity(mesh, &accepted, &mut fit);
651	        let comp = flood::<S>(mesh, in_region, start, &fit, centroids, vtol, stol);
652	        if comp.is_empty() {
653	            return None; // the start face does not lie on its own fit — reject
654	        }
655	        if comp.len() == accepted.len() && round > 0 {
656	            accepted = comp;
657	            break;
658	        }
659	        if comp.len() == prev {
660	            // Oscillating between two sizes — stop at the larger.
661	            if comp.len() >= accepted.len() {
662	                accepted = comp;
663	            }
664	            break;
665	        }
666	        prev = accepted.len();
667	        accepted = comp;
668	    }
669	
670	    // Final fit + validated flood.
671	    let verts = vertices_of(mesh, &accepted);
672	    let mut fit = S::fit_surface(mesh, &accepted, &verts, config.verbosity)?;
673	    S::determine_convexity(mesh, &accepted, &mut fit);
674	    let faces = flood::<S>(mesh, in_region, start, &fit, centroids, vtol, stol);
675	    if faces.len() < min_faces {
676	        return None;
677	    }
678	    let area: f64 = faces.iter().map(|&fi| face_area(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices)).sum();
679	    let verts = vertices_of(mesh, &faces);
680	    let hyp = S::make_hypothesis(mesh, faces.clone(), verts, &fit);
681	    Some((hyp, area, faces))
682	}
683	
684	
685	/// A committed torus's minor radius may not exceed this fraction of its major
686	/// radius. Real fillets and bend tubes are slim (r/R ≤ 0.5 across the corpus:
687	/// full donut 0.3, pipe-elbow 0.5, fillets 0.15–0.2); an r ≈ R "fat" torus is
688	/// a bogus fit a flat cap or wall band accepts loosely and then wins on area
689	/// (filleted_pipe: an r/R = 0.82 torus ate an annular end cap).
690	const MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO: f64 = 0.7;
691	
692	/// How far a support sweeps around an axis, in radians: the angular extent of
693	/// \`verts\` about the line through \`origin\` along \`axis\`, taken as 2π minus the
694	/// largest angular gap between consecutive vertices, so a patch straddling the
695	/// angle origin measures correctly.
696	///
697	/// This is the observable that says whether a rotational parameter is
698	/// determined by the data at all. A surface of revolution fitted to a patch
699	/// that never goes around its axis has its radius free, and a free parameter is
700	/// worse than useless in competition: the fit becomes a super-model of the
701	/// simpler surface it should have lost to — covering everything that surface
702	/// covers, then flooding a little further on the slack — so it takes the
703	/// largest-validated-area contest every time. See \`MIN_TORUS_SWEEP_DEG\` and
704	/// \`MIN_CYLINDER_SWEEP_DEG\`.
705	fn axial_sweep(
706	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
707	    verts: &[usize],
708	    origin: &[f64; 3],
709	    axis: &[f64; 3],
710	) -> f64 {
711	    let (u, w) = super::perpendicular_basis(axis);
712	    let mut angles: Vec<f64> = verts
713	        .iter()
714	        .filter_map(|&vi| {
715	            let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
716	            let d = [v.x - origin[0], v.y - origin[1], v.z - origin[2]];
717	            let (x, y) = (
718	                d[0] * u[0] + d[1] * u[1] + d[2] * u[2],
719	                d[0] * w[0] + d[1] * w[1] + d[2] * w[2],
720	            );
721	            // A vertex on the axis has no meaningful angle.
722	            (x.hypot(y) > 1e-12).then(|| y.atan2(x))
723	        })
724	        .collect();
725	    if angles.len() < 2 {
726	        return 0.0;
727	    }
728	    angles.sort_by(|p, q| p.partial_cmp(q).unwrap());
729	    let mut gap = angles[0] + std::f64::consts::TAU - angles[angles.len() - 1];
730	    for pair in angles.windows(2) {
731	        gap = gap.max(pair[1] - pair[0]);
732	    }
733	    (std::f64::consts::TAU - gap).max(0.0)
734	}
735	
736	/// Sweep of a candidate's support about the axis whose radius it determines.
737	/// Planes and spheres have no axis — a sphere's radius is pinned by any patch —
738	/// so they report a full turn and are never rejected on this ground.
739	fn candidate_sweep(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, hyp: &CompeteHyp) -> f64 {
740	    match hyp {
741	        CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h) => torus_sweep(mesh, h),
742	        CompeteHyp::Conical(h) => conical_sweep(mesh, h),
743	        CompeteHyp::Planar(_) | CompeteHyp::Spherical(_) => std::f64::consts::TAU,
744	    }
745	}
746	
747	/// Sweep of a toroidal hypothesis's support about its own axis.
748	fn torus_sweep(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, hyp: &ToroidalHypothesis) -> f64 {
749	    axial_sweep(mesh, &hyp.vertices, &hyp.center, &hyp.axis_direction)
750	}
751	
752	/// Sweep of a cylinder's support about its own axis. Meaningless for a true
753	/// cone, whose apex pins the axis regardless of sweep, so cones report a full
754	/// turn.
755	fn conical_sweep(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, hyp: &ConicalHypothesis) -> f64 {
756	    if !hyp.is_cylinder() {
757	        return std::f64::consts::TAU;
758	    }
759	    axial_sweep(mesh, &hyp.vertices, &hyp.apex, &hyp.axis_direction)
760	}
761	
762	/// Grow a toroidal candidate from an initial fit patch, trying flood starts
763	/// in order until one grows. Rejects degenerate giant-major-radius fits: a
764	/// short segment of straight cylinder fits a torus with an absurdly distant
765	/// axis (observed R ≈ 1500–3600 mm on ~100 mm models under the old BFS), and
766	/// a legitimate torus's major radius cannot exceed the mesh extent — the
767	/// same guard as the ladder's acceptance test. Also rejects "fat" tori
768	/// (\`r > MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO·R\`). The sweep guard that rejects a torus too
769	/// straight to have a major radius at all is applied to every candidate type
770	/// alike, in \`compete_region\` (see \`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`).
771	fn torus_candidate(
772	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
773	    in_region: &[bool],
774	    initial: &[usize],
775	    starts: &[usize],
776	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
777	    config: &Config,
778	) -> Option<(ToroidalHypothesis, f64, Vec<usize>)> {
779	    let bb_diag = bounding_box_diagonal(&mesh.vertices);
780	    for &start in starts.iter().take(MAX_TORUS_STARTS) {
781	        if let Some((hyp, area, faces)) = grow_from::<ToroidalSurface>(
782	            mesh,
783	            in_region,
784	            start,
785	            initial.to_vec(),
786	            centroids,
787	            config,
788	            MIN_TORUS_FACES,
789	        ) {
790	            // A grow that succeeded but produced an implausible torus says the
791	            // *type* is wrong here, so stop — retrying other flood starts from
792	            // the same fit patch would only rediscover it. Extra starts are
793	            // for the case where the grow itself failed.
794	            return (hyp.major_radius <= bb_diag
795	                && hyp.minor_radius <= MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO * hyp.major_radius)
796	            .then_some((hyp, area, faces));
797	        }
798	    }
799	    None
800	}
801	
802	/// Stage 2.3(c) bent-tube promotion. A cylinder flood that stalled against
803	/// region faces whose jet continues the tube — κ1 ≈ the tube's 1/r, |κ2|
804	/// well below κ1 (the same tube swinging around a bend, not a cap plane's
805	/// (0,0) or an end sphere's (κ,κ)) — is the developable prefix of a bent
806	/// tube. Fit a torus to the pure-bend patch just past the stall line
807	/// (frontier faces plus their off-cylinder tube-jet neighbours) and grow it;
808	/// the result competes on validated area like any other candidate.
809	///
810	/// The cylinder fit itself locates the tangency — frontier faces failed
811	/// cylinder validation — so unlike a jet-class seed patch (which cannot tell
812	/// bend from straight tube: both read the same κ1) this patch cannot
813	/// straddle the junction.
814	fn promote_bend(
815	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
816	    in_region: &[bool],
817	    cyl: &ConicalHypothesis,
818	    cyl_faces: &[usize],
819	    jet: &[JetField],
820	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
821	    config: &Config,
822	) -> Option<(ToroidalHypothesis, f64, Vec<usize>)> {
823	    if cyl.radius_at_apex <= 0.0 {
824	        return None;
825	    }
826	    let k_tube = 1.0 / cyl.radius_at_apex;
827	    let grow_vtol = config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
828	
829	    // Max vertex distance to the stalled cylinder — the "past the tangency"
830	    // test (frontier faces failed the flood at exactly this tolerance).
831	    let off_cyl_dist = |fi: usize| -> f64 {
832	        let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
833	        let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
834	        face.vertex_indices[..vc]
835	            .iter()
836	            .map(|&vi| {
837	                super::cylindrical::vertex_to_cylinder_distance(
838	                    &mesh.vertices[vi],
839	                    &cyl.apex,
840	                    &cyl.axis_direction,
841	                    cyl.radius_at_apex,
842	                )
843	                .abs()
844	            })
845	            .fold(0.0_f64, f64::max)
846	    };
847	    // Tube-continuation jet signature. The κ2 bound is generous: a bend's
848	    // inner (saddle) side reads |κ2| = 1/(R−r), which approaches κ1 = 1/r
849	    // only for very tight bends; junction rows have contaminated jets and
850	    // are excluded from the fit patch either way (the flood claims them).
851	    let tube_jet = |fi: usize| -> bool {
852	        let a1 = jet[fi].k1.abs();
853	        a1 >= 0.5 * k_tube && a1 <= 2.0 * k_tube && jet[fi].k2.abs() <= 0.8 * a1
854	    };
855	
856	    // Frontier: in-region neighbours of the cylinder support that are past
857	    // the tangency and continue the tube.
858	    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = cyl_faces.iter().copied().collect();
859	    let mut frontier: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
860	    for &fi in cyl_faces {
861	        let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
862	        for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
863	            if ni < 0 {
864	                continue;
865	            }
866	            let ni = ni as usize;
867	            if !in_region[ni] || !seen.insert(ni) {
868	                continue;
869	            }
870	            if off_cyl_dist(ni) > grow_vtol && tube_jet(ni) {
871	                frontier.push(ni);
872	            }
873	        }
874	    }
875	    if frontier.is_empty() {
876	        return None; // finished a straight pipe (or stalled at a cap/sphere)
877	    }
878	    frontier.sort_unstable();
879	
880	    // Pure-bend fit patch: depth-limited multi-source BFS from the frontier
881	    // through off-cylinder tube-jet faces. (Neighbours examined and rejected
882	    // above stay excluded — junction rows with contaminated jets would only
883	    // pollute the fit.)
884	    let mut patch: Vec<usize> = frontier.clone();
885	    let mut ring = frontier.clone();
886	    for _depth in 0..BEND_PATCH_DEPTH {
887	        if patch.len() >= BEND_PATCH_CAP {
888	            break;
889	        }
890	        let mut next: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
891	        for &fi in &ring {
892	            let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
893	            for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
894	                if ni < 0 {
895	                    continue;
896	                }
897	                let ni = ni as usize;
898	                if !in_region[ni] || seen.contains(&ni) {
899	                    continue;
900	                }
901	                if off_cyl_dist(ni) <= grow_vtol || !tube_jet(ni) {
902	                    continue;
903	                }
904	                seen.insert(ni);
905	                patch.push(ni);
906	                next.push(ni);
907	                if patch.len() >= BEND_PATCH_CAP {
908	                    break;
909	                }
910	            }
911	        }
912	        if next.is_empty() {
913	            break;
914	        }
915	        ring = next;
916	    }
917	    patch.sort_unstable();
918	
919	    // Flood starts: deepest-off-cylinder first — the faces most surely
920	    // interior to the bend (frontier faces sit on the junction row and may
921	    // not validate the torus fit themselves).
922	    let mut starts = patch.clone();
923	    starts.sort_by(|&a, &b| {
924	        off_cyl_dist(b)
925	            .partial_cmp(&off_cyl_dist(a))
926	            .unwrap()
927	            .then_with(|| a.cmp(&b))
928	    });
929	
930	    torus_candidate(mesh, in_region, &patch, &starts, centroids, config)
931	}
932	
933	/// Connected region-faces reachable from \`seed\` that all validate \`fit\`
934	/// (vertex tolerance + centroid tolerance + orientation coherence).
935	fn flood<S: SurfaceType>(
936	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
937	    in_region: &[bool],
938	    seed: usize,
939	    fit: &S::Fit,
940	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
941	    vtol: f64,
942	    stol: f64,
943	) -> Vec<usize> {
944	    if !face_validates::<S>(mesh, seed, fit, centroids, vtol, stol) {
945	        return Vec::new();
946	    }
947	    let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
948	    seen.insert(seed);
949	    let mut out = Vec::new();
950	    let mut stack = vec![seed];
951	    while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
952	        out.push(fi);
953	        let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
954	        for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
955	            if ni < 0 {
956	                continue;
957	            }
958	            let ni = ni as usize;
959	            if in_region[ni]
960	                && !seen.contains(&ni)
961	                && face_validates::<S>(mesh, ni, fit, centroids, vtol, stol)
962	            {
963	                seen.insert(ni);
964	                stack.push(ni);
965	            }
966	        }
967	    }
968	    out.sort_unstable();
969	    out
970	}
971	
972	/// Does face \`fi\` validate \`fit\` (all vertices ≤ vtol, centroid ≤ stol,
973	/// orientation coherent)?
974	fn face_validates<S: SurfaceType>(
975	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
976	    fi: usize,
977	    fit: &S::Fit,
978	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
979	    vtol: f64,
980	    stol: f64,
981	) -> bool {
982	    if !S::convexity_check(fit, mesh, fi) {
983	        return false;
984	    }
985	    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
986	    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
987	    for &vi in &face.vertex_indices[..vc] {
988	        let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
989	        if S::vertex_distance(fit, &[v.x, v.y, v.z]) > vtol {
990	            return false;
991	        }
992	    }
993	    S::centroid_distance(fit, &centroids[fi]) <= stol
994	}
995	
996	/// Max distance from any vertex of face \`fi\` to \`fit\` — the trim criterion for
997	/// conditioning the seed patch (\`condition_patch\`).
998	fn face_max_vertex_dist<S: SurfaceType>(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, fit: &S::Fit) -> f64 {
999	    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
1000	    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
1001	    face.vertex_indices[..vc]
1002	        .iter()
1003	        .map(|&vi| {
1004	            let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
1005	            S::vertex_distance(fit, &[v.x, v.y, v.z])
1006	        })
1007	        .fold(0.0_f64, f64::max)
1008	}
1009	
1010	/// Condition the seed patch so the seed lies on the patch's own fit. A
1011	/// depth-limited jet-similar patch can bleed a row or two across a tangent
1012	/// junction between two surfaces of the same curvature class — a short
1013	/// cylinder wall into its fillet, where the transition faces' κ1 ramps from
1014	/// the wall's toward the fillet's — which pulls the fit off far enough that
1015	/// the seed fails its own fit and the grow gives up (leaving the wall to be
1016	/// eaten by an over-grown torus). Trim the worst-fitting patch face (never the
1017	/// seed) and refit until the seed validates, bottoming out at a size floor so
1018	/// the fit stays well-determined. Fit-validated, in the spirit of "validation
1019	/// decides geometry"; a no-op when the patch is already clean.
1020	///
1021	/// When the patch admits no fit of this type at all there is no residual to
1022	/// rank by, so the round instead drops the face geometrically farthest from
1023	/// the seed: a straddling patch is unfittable precisely because its far end
1024	/// sits on another surface, and shrinking toward the seed is the one move that
1025	/// is monotonically local.
1026	#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
1027	fn condition_patch<S: SurfaceType>(
1028	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
1029	    seed: usize,
1030	    mut patch: Vec<usize>,
1031	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
1032	    config: &Config,
1033	    vtol: f64,
1034	    stol: f64,
1035	    min_faces: usize,
1036	) -> Vec<usize> {
1037	    let floor = min_faces.max(MIN_CONDITION_FACES);
1038	    for _ in 0..PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS {
1039	        if patch.len() <= floor {
1040	            break;
1041	        }
1042	        let verts = vertices_of(mesh, &patch);
1043	        let mut fit = match S::fit_surface(mesh, &patch, &verts, config.verbosity) {
1044	            Some(f) => f,
1045	            None => {
1046	                // No fit to rank by — shrink toward the seed instead.
1047	                let far = patch
1048	                    .iter()
1049	                    .copied()
1050	                    .filter(|&f| f != seed)
1051	                    .max_by(|&a, &b| {
1052	                        centroid_gap(centroids, seed, a)
1053	                            .partial_cmp(&centroid_gap(centroids, seed, b))
1054	                            .unwrap()
1055	                    });
1056	                match far {
1057	                    Some(f) => {
1058	                        patch.retain(|&p| p != f);
1059	                        continue;
1060	                    }
1061	                    None => break,
1062	                }
1063	            }
1064	        };
1065	        S::determine_convexity(mesh, &patch, &mut fit);
1066	        if face_validates::<S>(mesh, seed, &fit, centroids, vtol, stol) {
1067	            break;
1068	        }
1069	        let worst = patch
1070	            .iter()
1071	            .copied()
1072	            .filter(|&f| f != seed)
1073	            .max_by(|&a, &b| {
1074	                face_max_vertex_dist::<S>(mesh, a, &fit)
1075	                    .partial_cmp(&face_max_vertex_dist::<S>(mesh, b, &fit))
1076	                    .unwrap()
1077	            });
1078	        match worst {
1079	            Some(w) => patch.retain(|&f| f != w),
1080	            None => break,
1081	        }
1082	    }
1083	    patch
1084	}
1085	
1086	/// Distance between two faces' centroids.
1087	fn centroid_gap(centroids: &[[f64; 3]], a: usize, b: usize) -> f64 {
1088	    let (p, q) = (centroids[a], centroids[b]);
1089	    ((p[0] - q[0]).powi(2) + (p[1] - q[1]).powi(2) + (p[2] - q[2]).powi(2)).sqrt()
1090	}

1189	fn curvature_class(jf: JetField) -> CurvClass {
1190	    // Magnitude-ordered so a concave feature (dominant curvature negative, in
1191	    // k2) is not misread — see the note in \`compete_region\`.
1192	    let a1 = jf.k1.abs().max(jf.k2.abs());
1193	    let a2 = jf.k1.abs().min(jf.k2.abs());
1194	    // Absolute floor is generous; only used to cluster the seed patch.
1195	    if a1 < 1e-4 {
1196	        CurvClass::Flat
1197	    } else if a2 >= 0.4 * a1 {
1198	        CurvClass::Spherelike
1199	    } else {
1200	        CurvClass::Developable
1201	    }
1202	}

1204	/// Curvature mismatch of a face vs its region neighbors (low = interior).
1205	fn neighbor_jet_mismatch(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, jet: &[JetField], in_region: &[bool]) -> f64 {
1206	    let jf = jet[fi];
1207	    let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
1208	    let mut worst = 0.0_f64;
1209	    for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
1210	        if ni < 0 {
1211	            continue;
1212	        }
1213	        let ni = ni as usize;
1214	        if !in_region[ni] {
1215	            continue;
1216	        }
1217	        let jn = jet[ni];
1218	        let d = (jf.k1 - jn.k1).abs() + (jf.k2 - jn.k2).abs();
1219	        worst = worst.max(d);
1220	    }
1221	    worst
1222	}

1278	/// Largest bounding-box dimension of a region's vertices.
1279	fn region_extent(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, region: &Region) -> f64 {
1280	    let mut min = [f64::INFINITY; 3];
1281	    let mut max = [f64::NEG_INFINITY; 3];
1282	    for &vi in &region.vertices {

1284	        for (k, c) in [v.x, v.y, v.z].into_iter().enumerate() {
1285	            min[k] = min[k].min(c);
1286	            max[k] = max[k].max(c);
1287	        }
1288	    }
1289	    (max[0] - min[0]).max(max[1] - min[1]).max(max[2] - min[2])

243	pub(super) fn fit_cylinder(
244	    face_indices: &[usize],
245	    vertex_set: &[usize],
246	    faces: &[MeshFace],
247	    vertices: &[MeshVertex],
248	) -> Option<([f64; 3], [f64; 3], f64)> {
249	    if vertex_set.len() < 3 {
250	        return None;
251	    }
252	
253	    // Axis from smallest eigenvector of area-weighted normal covariance.
254	    let cov = build_normal_covariance(face_indices, faces, vertices);
255	    let mut axis_dir = smallest_eigenvector_3x3(&cov);
256	    let len = normalize3(&mut axis_dir);
257	    if len < 1e-15 {
258	        return None;
259	    }
260	
261	    // Initial circle fit to get starting origin and radius.
262	    let (axis_origin, radius) = fit_circle_for_axis(&axis_dir, vertex_set, vertices)?;
263	
264	    // Recenter vertices around their centroid for numerical stability.
265	    let n_pts = vertex_set.len() as f64;
266	    let mut centroid = [0.0_f64; 3];
267	    for &vi in vertex_set.iter() {
268	        centroid[0] += vertices[vi].x;
269	        centroid[1] += vertices[vi].y;
270	        centroid[2] += vertices[vi].z;
271	    }
272	    centroid[0] /= n_pts;
273	    centroid[1] /= n_pts;
274	    centroid[2] /= n_pts;
275	
276	    let points: Vec<[f64; 3]> = vertex_set.iter()
277	        .map(|&vi| [
278	            vertices[vi].x - centroid[0],
279	            vertices[vi].y - centroid[1],
280	            vertices[vi].z - centroid[2],
281	        ])
282	        .collect();
283	    let centered_origin = [
284	        axis_origin[0] - centroid[0],
285	        axis_origin[1] - centroid[1],
286	        axis_origin[2] - centroid[2],
287	    ];
288	
289	    // Refine with Levenberg-Marquardt optimization on radial residuals.
290	    let problem = CylinderLMProblem::new(points, axis_dir, centered_origin, radius);
291	    let (result, _report) = LevenbergMarquardt::new().with_patience(150).minimize(problem);
292	    let refined_dir = result.axis_dir_from_params(&result.params);
293	    let refined_origin = [

633	### Rewriting stage 2.3(b): the blind patch is the root cause
634	
635	*Design proposed 2026-07-28 from a session of measurements; not yet built. Every
636	claim below has a number behind it, and the dead ends are recorded so they are
637	not re-derived.*
638	
639	**Root cause.** \`grow_from\` seeds a hypothesis by fitting to an *unvalidated*
640	BFS neighbourhood (\`seed_patch\` → \`condition_patch\`, depth 3, up to 32 faces).
641	The patch never survives into the hypothesis — after round 0 it is replaced by
642	the flood output, so every committed face has passed \`face_validates\`. What
643	survives is its influence on the *initial fit parameters*, and that is enough to
644	produce both observed failures:
645	
646	1. The seed fails its own contaminated fit → \`flood\` returns empty → the
647	   candidate is rejected. 323 such events on \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine.
648	2. Or the contaminated fit is merely *plausible*, and the flood validates a set
649	   of faces against the wrong surface. Every member genuinely lies within
650	   tolerance of it.
651	
652	So validation is working perfectly and is not protecting us: the membership test
653	is sound, the thing being tested against is wrong. That is also why "prune to
654	what validates" is nearly a no-op — there is nothing unvalidated to prune.
655	
656	**What survived this session's measurements, and is what the rework should lean
657	on.** Validation by vertex distance always behaved. The whole-region DOF ladder
658	is exact when the region is right (sharp-edged models need no tuning at all).
659	The per-facet fallback is *geometrically exact* for single-facet surfaces — 14
660	of this model's 16 planes arrive that way and are correct. Largest-validated-area
661	selection works (all 18 tori exact, 34/37 cylinders). The junction locus is exact
662	and verified corpus-wide. Refitting on corrected support is what buys accuracy.
663	
664	**What did not survive — four local per-face signals, all measured wrong. Do not
665	re-derive.** Relative *face size* is anti-correlated with junctions (across a
666	flat|curved junction the median size ratio is 1.26; *within* a plane it is p90
667	47.5, because Delaunay grading rings a plane with slivers finer than the fillet
668	facets it abuts). The strip *long edge* is not reliably the ruling (adjacent
669	strips' long directions differ by up to 76°). A ring-local **shape operator**
670	from neighbour normals is worse than the jet on 155:1 strips and rank-deficient
671	on a quad whose four neighbours sample only two directions. And *dimensionless*
672	curvature κ·extent does not separate either — the plane quad sits at 0.139
673	against fillet strips at 2.10, inside a continuum.
674	
675	**Status: steps 0, 1, 3 and 4 are built; step 5 is measured NOT YET POSSIBLE (2026-07-28).**
676	
677	- **2.3(d), the leftover-component ladder rescue** (\`rescue_leftover_components\`,
678	  classify.rs). A leftover connected component *is* a region competition failed
679	  to explain, so the whole-region DOF ladder runs on each one before freeform
680	  gets it. No new math was needed: the conical fitter already estimates its axis
681	  as the smallest eigenvector of the normal covariance, and given an
682	  uncontaminated face set recovers r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 against true
683	  1.0 / 1.0 / 0.5. Competition was simply never handing it a clean face set.
684	  It can only *add* surfaces — every rung is vertex-tolerance validated, so a
685	  genuinely freeform component still falls through to 2.3(e).
686	- **Visited ≠ claimed** (compete.rs). Committing no longer retires the faces a
687	  hypothesis grew into; every region face seeds exactly once. Faces in the
688	  *interior* of the explained set are skipped, because a fit grown from the
689	  middle of a validated surface merely refits that surface — a pragmatic bound,
690	  not a derived one. Reseeding with no bound at all was measured at over ten
691	  minutes on \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine and abandoned; the bounded form costs
692	  42 s → 103 s.
693	
694	- **2.3(d2), junction-constrained growth** (\`grow_with_axis\` in compete.rs,
695	  \`rescue_with_neighbour_axes\` in classify.rs). Borrow the axis direction from
696	  an adjacent committed surface and grow a cylinder with it fixed, largest
697	  validated area first, iterating until nothing more grows. With the axis fixed
698	  the fit is a 3-DOF circle in the perpendicular plane — well conditioned even
699	  on a short arc — so growth starts from the seed facet alone and there is no
700	  blind patch to straddle. This is what closes the leftover blob that 2.3(d)
701	  cannot: a blob spanning several fillets validates no single ladder rung, but
702	  it is bounded by surfaces that are already right.
703	
704	Together: that model went **138 → 75 surfaces** (planes 80 → 18 against a
705	reference 16, cylinders 34 → 38 against 37, **tori 21 → 18, exactly the
706	reference**, B-splines 3 → 1), with the other 13 probe models bit-identical.
707	
708	Superseded numbers from the intermediate state: after steps 0–1 alone it was
709	**138 → 95 surfaces** (cylinders 34 → 36 against a
710	reference 37, tori 21 → 19 against 18, B-splines 3 → 1, per-facet planes
711	80 → 39), with the other 13 probe models bit-identical.
712	
713	- **Step 4, the (d)/(d2) fixpoint.** The two rescues alternate until neither
714	  commits anything: (d2) splitting a blob can leave a remainder one ladder rung
715	  explains, and (d) committing a surface gives (d2) another axis to borrow.
716	  Terminating, since every round strictly shrinks the leftovers. **Measured
717	  inert on the corpus** — the two already converge in one round on every model
718	  — so it is a guarantee rather than a fix, kept because the cost is one extra
719	  pass over an unchanged set.
720	
721	**Step 5 — deleting \`seed_patch\`/\`condition_patch\`/\`PATCH_*\` — is NOT a cleanup,
722	and is not yet possible. Measured.** Cutting the blind patch to the seed alone
723	(\`SEED_PATCH_CAP\` = 1) collapses \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine to **24872
724	surfaces**, essentially one per facet. The blind patch is still the sole source
725	of initial parameters for the *free* fit path, which commits ~63 hypotheses
726	before either rescue runs; (d) and (d2) only clean up after it. Deleting it
727	requires the constrained path to take over the primary role — every surface
728	grown on an axis borrowed from a committed neighbour, bootstrapped from
729	somewhere other than a blind neighbourhood — which is a further design step, not
730	a removal. The stage-2 constant count therefore stays at 34.
731	
732	**What the remaining 24-surface gap now is** (measured after steps 0–1, on
733	\`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine: 95 against a reference 71). Twenty-three of the
734	twenty-four surplus surfaces are **single-facet planes** — leftover slivers that
735	nothing explained — plus one extra torus, one spurious B-spline, one missing
736	cylinder. Two distinct kinds of sliver, and they want different fixes:
737	
738	- *Absorbable ones.* Face 6758 sits 7.7e-5 mm from an adjacent committed
739	  \`cyl r=1.0000\` and 4.1e-5 from an adjacent single-facet plane — the first is
740	  1.5× vertex tolerance, the second *inside* it. These are boundary faces that
741	  stage 2.4's relabel/merge/snap ought to take, and mostly the repair pass does
742	  reduce 140 committed surfaces to 95.
743	- *Unfitted curved runs.* Faces 9857/9866 and their neighbours are a chain of
744	  per-facet planes whose normals rotate progressively — n = [0,−0.280,0.960],
745	  [0,−0.269,0.963], [0,−0.248,0.969], [0,−0.238,0.971], [0,−0.227,0.974],
746	  [0,−0.206,0.979] — with adjacent-facet distances of 4.7e-4 to 1.4e-3 mm, far
747	  above vertex tolerance. That is a fillet arc that was never fitted as a
748	  cylinder, sitting in per-facet pieces.
749	
750	**Limitation of 2.3(d) found doing this, and it shapes step 3.** The rescue
751	fits *one* surface per leftover component. After steps 0–1 the region still
752	leaves 104 leftovers whose largest connected blob is **49 faces**, and that blob
753	spans several distinct fillets — so no single ladder rung validates it and the
754	whole blob falls through. Re-running competition inside it does not help either,
755	since visited ≠ claimed already seeded every one of those faces. What such a
756	blob has that competition lacks is *committed neighbours*: it is bounded by
757	surfaces that are already correct, which is exactly the junction constraint of
758	step 3. Expect step 3 to be what closes this, not more seeding.
759	
760	**The design (remaining steps).**
761	
762	- **Seeding stays universal.** Every unclaimed face is a candidate seed; the
763	  constraint machinery below is an addition, never a fallback.
764	- **Visited ≠ claimed.** Growing into a face is a hypothesis, not a conclusion.
765	  A seed that commits nothing costs one entry from the untried list and nothing
766	  else; the faces it touched stay available. This is what makes an unreliable
767	  bootstrap survivable — a 36-face surface gets 36 independent attempts — and it
768	  makes the plan's long-standing claim that seed misordering "costs only time"
769	  actually true, where today an early bad hypothesis consumes faces a later,
770	  better seed needed.
771	- **The commit gate is IDENTIFIABILITY, not residual.** Measured and important:
772	  the three spurious tori on \`stepped_block_toroidal\` fine fit their 7–8 faces
773	  to **0.52 / 0.72 / 0.87 mesh quanta**, against genuine surfaces at mean 0.39
774	  and max 0.56. They overlap — a 7-DOF torus on 7 faces is essentially exactly
775	  determined and fits perfectly by construction. No residual or coverage test
776	  can reject them, exactly as *Identifiability is not model selection* says. The
777	  gate must ask whether the support pins the parameters (\`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`
778	  and the conditioning it stands for), and the quantum belongs where it did
779	  work — as the *unit* for judging a fit whose parameters are already pinned.
780	- **Expand under junction constraints where a neighbour is already committed.**
781	  This is what \`locus.rs\` unlocked. Measured on the three cylinders this model
782	  loses: each is a fillet-chain segment sitting between committed neighbours of
783	  *exactly its own tube radius* (cyl r=1.0 / cyl r=1.0; torus r=1.0 / cyl r=1.0;
784	  torus r=0.5 / cyl r=0.5). Fixing only the axis direction recovers
785	  **r = 0.99998, 0.99999, 0.50008** from arcs of 65°, 60° and 40°, with maximum
786	  residuals of 1.0e-5, 1.0e-5 and 5.0e-6 mm — five to ten times *under* vertex
787	  tolerance. The free 5-DOF fit fails on the **axis**, not the radius, and the
788	  neighbour supplies exactly that.
789	- **Iterate seed passes to a fixpoint**, so a segment that cannot be fitted
790	  freely on pass 1 becomes a nearly-constrained problem on pass 2 once its
791	  neighbour is committed. Terminating: each pass either commits a face or ends.
792	
793	**What it deletes.** \`seed_patch\`, \`condition_patch\`, \`grow_patch\`,
794	\`SEED_PATCH_CAP\`, \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`, \`PATCH_RADII\`, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\`,
795	\`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`, and the jet's role in seeding (leaving it to the ladder's
796	rung ordering). The flood is sound and stays.
797	
798	**The open risk, stated narrowly.** Not "a model with no anchor" — seeding stays

1	//! Stage 2.3(a): the whole-region DOF ladder.
2	//!
3	//! Try to explain each smooth region (stage 2.2) with ONE surface, ascending
4	//! degrees of freedom; the first fit that validates wins. Simplest-first
5	//! resolves degeneracy (any plane also lies on an infinite-radius cylinder; a
6	//! single facet is exactly a plane) without coverage heuristics.
7	//!
8	//! | Rung | Surface  | DOF |
9	//! |------|----------|-----|
10	//! | 1    | Plane    | 3   |
11	//! | 2    | Sphere   | 4   |
12	//! | 3    | Conical  | 5/6 | (cylinder-or-cone; the fitter prefers cylinder)
13	//! | 4    | Torus    | 7   |
14	//!
15	//! The conical rung fits a cylinder first and only falls to a cone when the
16	//! cylinder does not validate (\`ConicalSurface::fit_surface\`), so it covers
17	//! both the 5-DOF cylinder and 6-DOF cone cases in DOF order.
18	//!
19	//! **Jet-informed order between the sphere and conical rungs.** Strict
20	//! simplest-first breaks on a short cylinder/cone tessellated as a few rings:
21	//! two same-radius rings at heights ±h lie exactly on a sphere of radius
22	//! √(R²+h²), so the lower-DOF sphere would validate and wrongly win. The jet
23	//! resolves it — a developable region (median \`min/max\` principal-curvature
24	//! ratio ~0) tries the conical rung before the sphere rung; a bi-curved
25	//! region (ratio ~1) keeps sphere-first. The jet only ORDERS; a genuine
26	//! sphere it misreads still falls through to the sphere rung, since a
27	//! cylinder fit cannot validate a sphere's vertices (\`region_is_developable\`).
28	//!
29	//! **Validation decides all geometry** (Design Principle #1): every claimed
30	//! vertex within \`--vertex-tolerance\`, every claimed face centroid within
31	//! \`--surface-tolerance\`, and orientation coherence (every face normal
32	//! agrees in sign with the surface normal at its centroid — the per-type
33	//! \`convexity_check\`, which also fixes the hypothesis \`convex\` flag). The
34	//! fitters apply their own numeric-validity guards (cone half-angle bounds,
35	//! torus \`0 < r ≤ R\`, sphere radius cap) and return \`None\` outside them.
36	//!
37	//! A region the ladder cannot explain with one surface is a tangent chain
38	//! (pill capsule, rounded-cube shell, pipe-elbow tube); it goes to stage
39	//! 2.3(b)/(c) scoped competition (\`compete.rs\`). Whatever competition leaves
40	//! unclaimed **terminally degrades to per-facet planes** — total coverage
41	//! can never fail, and a single triangle is exactly a plane.
42

44	use crate::stage1::ConnectedMesh;

46	use super::surface::SurfaceType;
47	use super::bspline::{self, BSplineHypothesis};
48	use super::compete::{self, CompeteHyp};
49	use super::conical::ConicalSurface;
50	use super::edges::JetField;
51	use super::planar::{fit_plane, PlanarSurface};

56	    face_centroid, ConicalHypothesis, PlanarHypothesis, SelectedSurface, SphericalHypothesis,

58	};
59	
60	use std::collections::HashSet;
61	
62	/// Regions larger than this many faces are fitted on a deterministic
63	/// stride subsample (validated against the whole region, refit-augmented on
64	/// a near-miss). Bounds fit cost on huge regions — which, lacking scoped
65	/// competition, are the tangent chains that fail the ladder anyway.
66	const MAX_FIT_FACES: usize = 3000;
67	
68	/// Bounded refit rounds: after a subsample fit, the worst validation
69	/// offenders are added to the fit set and the surface is refit, so a large
70	/// clean surface still validates end to end.
71	const MAX_REFIT: usize = 3;
72	
73	/// How many worst-offender faces to fold into the fit set per refit round.
74	const REFIT_ADD: usize = 32;

77	/// this multiple of \`--surface-tolerance\`, the region is decisively not this

137	            CompeteHyp::Spherical(h) => self.push_spherical(h),
138	            CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h) => self.push_toroidal(h),
139	        }
140	    }
141	}

364	/// Fit surface type \`S\` to a region and validate it against the whole region.
365	/// Large regions fit on a stride subsample, refit-augmented with the worst
366	/// validation offenders on a near-miss. Returns the committed hypothesis, or
367	/// \`None\` if \`S\` cannot explain the region within tolerance.
368	///
369	/// Also the stage-2.4 merge revalidation entry point (\`repair.rs\`): refit a
370	/// candidate union and confirm it validates end to end before committing.
371	pub(super) fn fit_validate<S: SurfaceType>(
372	    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
373	    region_faces: &[usize],
374	    region_verts: &[usize],
375	    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
376	    config: &Config,
377	) -> Option<S::Hypothesis> {
378	    let vtol = config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
379	    let stol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
380	
381	    // Initial fit set: the whole region, or a deterministic stride subsample
382	    // when the region is large.
383	    let mut fit_faces: Vec<usize> = if region_faces.len() > MAX_FIT_FACES {
384	        let stride = region_faces.len().div_ceil(MAX_FIT_FACES);
385	        region_faces.iter().copied().step_by(stride).collect()
386	    } else {
387	        region_faces.to_vec()
388	    };
389	
390	    for _round in 0..=MAX_REFIT {
391	        let fit_verts = vertices_of(mesh, &fit_faces);
392	        let mut fit = S::fit_surface(mesh, &fit_faces, &fit_verts, config.verbosity)?;
393	        S::determine_convexity(mesh, region_faces, &mut fit);
394	
395	        // Validate every region face; collect near-miss offenders for a refit.
396	        let mut offenders: Vec<(f64, usize)> = Vec::new();
397	        for &fi in region_faces {
398	            // Orientation coherence: a face whose normal disagrees with the
399	            // fitted surface's convexity means the region is not one clean
400	            // surface of type S — no refit rescues that.
401	            if !S::convexity_check(&fit, mesh, fi) {
402	                return None;
403	            }
404	            let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
405	            let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
406	            let mut excess = 0.0_f64;
407	            for &vi in &face.vertex_indices[..vc] {
408	                let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
409	                let d = S::vertex_distance(&fit, &[v.x, v.y, v.z]);
410	                if d > vtol {
411	                    excess = excess.max(d - vtol);
412	                }
413	            }
414	            let cd = S::centroid_distance(&fit, &centroids[fi]);
415	            if cd > stol {
416	                excess = excess.max(cd - stol);
417	            }
418	            if excess > 0.0 {
419	                offenders.push((excess, fi));
420	            }
421	        }
422	
423	        if offenders.is_empty() {
424	            return Some(S::make_hypothesis(
425	                mesh,
426	                region_faces.to_vec(),
427	                region_verts.to_vec(),
428	                &fit,
429	            ));
430	        }
431	
432	        // Near-miss handling: if the worst offender is far out, the region is
433	        // decisively not this surface. Otherwise fold the worst offenders
434	        // into the fit set and refit (bounded rounds).
435	        offenders.sort_by(|a, b| {
436	            b.0.partial_cmp(&a.0)
437	                .unwrap()
438	                .then_with(|| a.1.cmp(&b.1))
439	        });
440	        if offenders[0].0 > REFIT_GIVE_UP * stol {
441	            return None;
442	        }
443	        let before = fit_faces.len();
444	        let existing: HashSet<usize> = fit_faces.iter().copied().collect();
445	        for &(_, fi) in offenders.iter().take(REFIT_ADD) {
446	            if !existing.contains(&fi) {
447	                fit_faces.push(fi);
448	            }
449	        }
450	        fit_faces.sort_unstable();
451	        fit_faces.dedup();
452	        if fit_faces.len() == before {
453	            // No new faces to learn from — cannot improve.
454	            return None;
455	        }
456	    }
457	    None
458	}

<!-- msg_011CdX1xK42hcr1kRbXuVtmR -->

I've read the TODO, the DEVELOPMENT_PLAN's blind-patch section, and the seeding/fitting code (`compete.rs`, `classify.rs`, the fitters). Here's the state of things, then a close look at your idea.

## Where we're at

The stage-2 rewrite is complete and the suite is at 513/9-ignored. The seeding rework (steps 0, 1, 3, 4) landed: leftover-component ladder rescue, visited≠claimed, borrowed-axis growth, and their fixpoint — `stepped_block_toroidal` fine went 138 → 75 surfaces against a reference 71. The NURBS corpus is fully covered end-to-end for the first time; 5 of 8 pass at 0.0 mm, with precise blockers on the other three (`hill_with_hole`'s one junk torus; `blister`/`saddle`'s pole-bound freeform fit failures). The explicit queue is: **(2) bound the runtime** (visited≠claimed cost 42 s → 103 s; the open question is how much of the 26122-face seed sweep does useful work), **(3) the last 4 surplus surfaces** on stepped_block fine, then the strategic arc **B-prime → B → C** (quantum-as-unit, estimate exporter tolerances from the mesh, VSA rewrite). Step 5 — deleting the blind patch — is measured **not yet possible**: it's still the sole source of initial parameters for the free fit path, and the prerequisite is exactly the bootstrap design problem you're raising. (Also: the TODO has an explicit "ASK THE USER" on whether to keep or delete the inert `snap_boundaries` — flagging that it's waiting on you.)

## First, a correction to the framing

Two premises in your question don't match what's measured:

**It's not unclear why single-face seeds fail — it's diagnosed end to end** (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN, "the blind patch is the root cause"). A single face can't determine a 5–7-DOF curved surface, so `grow` bootstraps from a blind 32-face BFS neighbourhood ([seed_patch](src/stage2/compete.rs:1107) → `condition_patch`). That patch never survives into the hypothesis, but its contamination lives on in the fit parameters — producing both the 323 NO-GROWTH seeds and the plausible-but-wrong fits whose members all genuinely validate.

**Interior seeds are already tried first, and it doesn't help.** Seed order is jet-interior-first (`neighbor_jet_mismatch` ascending), and visited≠claimed gives every region face a turn. The measured killer is that the *surfaces are narrower than the patch*: 72 of 75 flat components on stepped_block are under 32 faces, and of 200 sampled flat seeds — including perfectly interior ones — **zero** produced a patch that stayed on the flat. Seed position isn't the lever; patch construction is. And beneath that sits the sagitta arithmetic: over a 7.5° arc on r = 0.5 the sag is ~0.001 mm against a 2e-5 mm quantum, so *no* local patch, however chosen, can pin a free surface of revolution. Only removing DOF helps — which is why the borrowed-axis fit (3-DOF circle, exact on a 40° arc) worked.

## Your algorithm, piece by piece

**Decimated-vertex fitting: sound, and already built.** [fit_validate](src/stage2/classify.rs:371) fits regions over 3000 faces on a stride subsample, validates against the whole region, and refit-augments with the worst offenders. It's proven machinery. But note fit *cost* isn't the bottleneck — the 103 s goes into per-seed grow/flood/refit loops, not into least squares. Decimation also doesn't decontaminate: a subsampled straddling patch is still straddling.

**The top-down recursion: the top level already exists.** "Try to fit one surface to the whole patch, accept on perfect fit" is literally the whole-region DOF ladder (`classify_one_region`) — and it's the part of the pipeline that needs no tuning at all. Your proposal is really: when the ladder fails, replace the seed sweep with geometric bisection. That's the interesting part, and it has one genuinely right instinct and two serious problems.

**Right instinct:** big patches are where fits are identifiable. A 2×7 quad arc across 90° pins a cylinder to 1e-9 (`cylinder_fit_exact_on_partial_arc`), and a top-down search spends its attempts at large scales first — attacking exactly the "26122 seeds, mostly useless" cost that TODO item 2 names.

**Problem 1: cut lines don't respect seams.** The seam assumption says real boundaries are mesh-edge chains; bisection cuts are arbitrary. This corpus's surfaces are *strips* — 12-face planes between fillets, long thin fillet chains — so a straight cut crosses many surfaces, and a cell containing a strip stays contaminated at every level until the cell is smaller than the strip (~11 levels on stepped_block). Pieces of one surface scatter across sibling cells and need a merge phase to reassemble. Fixing this by refining assignments after cutting turns the algorithm into VSA — which is item C, already the plan.

**Problem 2: the recursion descends into the regime where fitting stops being informative.** Your terminal test "perfect fit → select" is unsound at small scale: a 7-DOF torus on 7 faces fits at 0.52–0.87 quanta, *overlapping* genuine surfaces (0.39 mean). Everything fits perfectly near the identifiability floor — that's how `hill_with_hole` got 40 junk cones/spheres, and the sweep guard can't help there because `candidate_sweep` is vacuous for cones and spheres. So subdivision bounds the worst case in *time* (O(N log N) fit attempts, a real improvement) but not in *quality*: on a freeform region it recurses to the floor and commits garbage unless commit is gated on identifiability, which is exactly step 2 of the existing rework plan ("identifiability as the commit gate, never residual").

**Your undecidable branch — "plausible fit within" vs "clearly no fit" — is right to worry you, and it has a name here:** it's the analytic-vs-B-spline model selection problem, on which **five formulations have failed** (the deprioritised item; do not re-derive them — fit quality, region fraction, leftover count, connected-leftover size, and count-based candidacy are all measured wrong). No single-patch statistic separates "junction inside, subdivide" from "freeform, stop": both fail the analytic fit by 10–100× vtol. Two signals *do* carry information, though: (a) failure **scale in quantum units** separates "this cell is one surface" (≤ ~1 q) from "it is not" (~80 q) — that's your first branch, and it's solid; (b) for the second branch, the workable move is to not decide it locally at all — always subdivide on structural failure, gate commits on identifiability, reassemble siblings by refit-union-validation (the existing merge criterion in repair.rs), and let freeform be the *fall-through* for whatever commits nothing identifiable. Sibling coherence is the real freeform detector: halves of a real cylinder refit to the same surface; neighbouring cells of a hill fit accidental cones that won't merge.

## Verdict and where it fits

The idea has merit — but as the **step-5 bootstrap and item C's initializer, not as a rival pipeline**. Concretely: a hierarchical pass whose cells, where they fit identifiably, become initial hypotheses at the right scale would replace the blind patch as the free path's parameter source (the exact open design problem blocking step 5), and its split/merge structure is a principled answer to C's stated gap, the proxy-count rule. Its weak form — fixed geometric cuts, commit-on-perfect-fit — re-derives two measured failure modes; its strong form — residual-guided refinement, identifiability-gated commits — *is* VSA with a deterministic initialization, which is where the plan was already heading. Also worth doing regardless, and cheap: instrument the current sweep for TODO item 2 (what fraction of committed hypotheses survive `resolve_claims`) — that number is simultaneously the runtime case and the measured case for replacing per-face seeding with hierarchical attempts. And on the true worst case: you already noted subdivision can't beat it when a NURBS surface is required — today that path is blocked even earlier, by the pole-bound freeform fit failures on `blister`/`saddle`, which the TODO marks as "fix the freeform fit first" no matter what seeding looks like.

## 🤖 Assistant

I've thought this through against the measured record, and saved the design state to memory. Here's my assessment.

## Verdict: right shape, wrong promotion schedule

The idea is sound, and I think it's the missing step-5 design — the "bootstrap for the free path that is not a blind neighbourhood" the TODO says is a design problem the size of the rework. It has a classical pedigree (this is Besl–Jain variable-order region growing, the canonical range-image segmentation family), and more tellingly, **the codebase already contains a working instance of it**: [promote_bend](src/stage2/compete.rs:814) is exactly a type promotion — a cylinder stalls, a torus is seeded from the faces just past the stall line, and it measurably works. Your proposal generalizes that one hand-built transition into the universal growth mechanism.

But the schedule "1 face = plane, 2 = cylinder, 3 = sphere/torus" has a flaw at its center: **face counts measure exact determination, and exact determination is the enemy here, not the goal.** Two adjacent quads (6 vertices) do determine a 5-DOF cylinder — and that's the trap. An exactly-determined fit validates its own support *by construction* (the measured spurious tori fit at 0.52–0.87 quanta, overlapping genuine surfaces at 0.39), while its extrapolation is garbage: the sagitta arithmetic says a 7.5° arc pins r only to ~2% ≈ 200× vtol. Worse, promotion is attempted at exactly the moment the data can't distinguish "this really is a cylinder" from "we hit the edge of a plane" — the measured impostor cylinders (a flat wall plus one fillet row, sweeping 2.8°) are precisely what naive promotion would mint at every tangent junction.

The fix is the machinery you already have: **promote on identifiability of the newly added DOF, never on face count.** A plane→cylinder promotion is legal when the support sweeps enough about the candidate axis (`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG`, applied at promotion instead of post-hoc commit — cheaper, since no wasted flood); cone promotion has `axis_normals_consistent` and the half-angle bounds; sphere promotion needs one new test (normal spread in two directions), because [candidate_sweep](src/stage2/compete.rs:739) is currently vacuous for spheres — which is exactly how `hill_with_hole`'s accidents got through.

## The design, sharpened: flood–stall–bridge

Working through the corpus cases forced one refinement that I think makes the whole thing cohere. There's an unavoidable gap between "the simpler type died" and "the richer type's parameters are pinned" — on a fine r=0.5 fillet, the plane dies at 2 rows (sag ≈ 4.4e-4 mm > vtol) but a cylinder isn't identifiable until ~20° ≈ 8 rows. Promotion can't be refuse-or-commit at that boundary; it has to be a **bridge**: grow under an exactly-determined, *uncommittable* fit, refitting after every accreted face and revalidating the *entire* support each step, until either the new DOF become identified (→ hand off to the existing `flood` for extent) or nothing more accretes (→ the bridge dies, and the last *identified* type at its own extent is the candidate). So:

- **Plane rung needs no bridge at all.** One facet is an exact plane; flood immediately. This alone fixes the measured 323 flat-seed NO-GROWTH failures — `condition_patch` giving up at `MIN_CONDITION_FACES` without checking the seed validates simply has no analogue here.
- **Stall → bridge to the next rung(s)**, seeded from the current validated support plus the stall frontier — never from a blind neighbourhood. The jet can still prune *which* bridges to attempt (prior, not gate).
- **Commit gates, largest-area selection, and `resolve_claims` stay untouched.** The bridge changes only where initial parameters come from. This respects the (d2) lesson — a locally-valid constrained fit must not commit on its own local contest — because only identified-and-flooded candidates ever reach selection.

Walking the hard cases: a seed on a flat grows the plane to the full flat (junction rows rejected face-by-face — rejects aren't fatal, they just don't accrete); the cylinder bridge then ingests one fillet row, but the next row breaks the huge-radius fit against the flat support, the bridge dies at 3° sweep, and the plane commits at its true extent *without* the junction row. A seed on a fine fillet dies as a plane at row 2, bridges as a cylinder through rows 3–8, identifies, floods the whole fillet, and beats the row-plane candidate on area. No case needs a new tuned constant.

## Why this is strong specifically where the blind patch is irreplaceable

The recorded "one real risk" of deleting the blind patch is the **coarse-mesh curved bootstrap** — where the jet reads κ₁ = 0.023/0.162/2.2 across one exact arc. The bridge design doesn't touch the jet, and coarse meshes are its *easy* case: dihedral steps up to 24° per row mean identifiability arrives in 2–3 accretions, and the per-row sag is large relative to the quantum, so the radius conditions *better* per face than on fine meshes. The thing that kills the blind patch there (32 faces straddle everything) is irrelevant to a grower that counts geometry, not faces. That inverts the risk profile of step 5.

Honest limits: the **torus bridge is the longest and most fragile** (7 DOF; keep `promote_bend` and (d2) as-is alongside). **Bridge soak** is real — an exactly-determined fit on a tiny support can absorb a junction face within tolerance — but the consequence is a dead attempt, not a wrong commit, and visited≠claimed already prices that at one seed's turn. And it does **nothing about freeform-vs-analytic accidents**: an identifiable 15-face sphere on a coarse freeform hill still commits, so the `hill_with_hole` junk-torus class is unchanged — that's the model-selection problem, orthogonal to seeding.

If it works, it deletes exactly step 5's list: `seed_patch`, `condition_patch`, `grow_patch`, `SEED_PATCH_CAP`, `MAX_PATCH_DEPTH`, `PATCH_RADII`, `PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS`, `MIN_CONDITION_FACES`, and the jet's seeding role — and it should *help* TODO item 2's runtime problem, since attempts die at face 2–3 instead of after fitting and 16-round-trimming a 32-face patch.

**Suggested first move:** a spike implementing only the cylinder bridge (plane-stall → accrete-refit-identify → flood), behind an env flag, measured with `tmp/probe.sh` against the 75-surface fine baseline — with `stepped_block_toroidal` medium and `part_rounded_cube` coarse as the validation targets, per the plan's own instruction.


## 👤 User

One more thing to ponder: finding surface boundaries at tangent edges is a challenge. A goal is to find seads that are far from tangent edges and thus maximally likely to be fully contained in one surface. If this were an image processing problem, I'd think about it as an edge-finding & blurring problem: compute pixel gradients to find edges, then blur until the edge data is diffused a good distance from the edges, then look for local maxima to identify points far from edges. Can we do something similar in facet-space?

> The user wants to apply a distance-from-edges concept to mesh facets — similar to how edge detection and diffusion work in image processing, but adapted for finding seed points that are far from tangent junctions. The goal is to place seeds in the interior of surfaces where they're most likely to belong to a single facet rather than near boundaries where multiple surfaces meet.
> 
> But here's the core problem: in facet-space, the "gradient" signal that would normally reveal edges doesn't exist. Tangent junctions are invisible by definition — normals stay continuous across them, which is what makes them tangent in the first place. The sharp edges are already handled separately through dihedral thresholding. So what would an edge detector even operate on?
> 
> The measured evidence here is pretty conclusive: curvature-based seam detection was tested extensively and failed. Curvature gradients (the second-order analogue of intensity gradients) don't separate real junctions from natural curvature variation — the classes overlap completely. This was the direct measurement from the 2026-07-20 pivot, and it's documented as a dead end.
> 
> Structural signals like grid-direction discontinuity or density changes were never even attempted because they're mesh-dependent and essentially absent in OCCT's Delaunay interiors. Four different local per-face signals all measured wrong: face size ratio actually anti-correlated, strip long-edge direction, ring-local shape operator, dimensionless curvature scaled by extent. The jet itself is unreliable on coarse meshes, and adjacent jet-flat faces show median dihedral angles around 1.24° on stepped_block — the jet's flat set doesn't actually correspond to the planes.
> 
> But the user's proposal is subtly different from trying to detect seams accurately. They just need a seed ordering — a scalar field where local maxima tend to be interior faces. The current approach already does this in spirit: ordering by neighbor jet mismatch ascending, which puts jet-interior faces first.
> 
> The user's blur-and-find-maxima approach adds a diffusion step: instead of ranking by raw local mismatch (noisy and only 1-ring deep), diffuse the edge signal so distance-from-edge information propagates outward, then seed at the maxima. This is really an upgrade to seed ordering rather than a detector, and the jet functions as a prior not a gate — misordering only costs time since visited≠claimed. Better ordering has concrete value: fewer wasted seeds and better bootstrap quality where interior seeds lead to early accretions staying on-surface and faster bridge identification.
> 
> Now I'm thinking through the right facet-space construction by mapping image-processing concepts to meshes. The key question is what signal to use for gradient or edge strength per edge or face. > 
> The measured curvature-threshold approach failed because no threshold cleanly separates junctions from real curvature gradients, but for ordering purposes I don't need perfect separation — just a relative field where junctions are "worse" than regions far from them. I could use jet mismatch between neighbors as a curvature-gradient magnitude, or fit a small quadric or plane per face neighborhood and use the residual as the signal.
> 
> For the blur or diffusion step on a mesh, I'm thinking about heat diffusion on the face-adjacency graph using the graph Laplacian with iterated neighbor operations. are essentially the medial axis of the partition itself — the watershed algorithm naturally finds these by flooding from local minima of the curvature field, treating edge strength as topography. This is a well-established approach in mesh segmentation, though it tends to over-segment due to noise, which is where the merging and relabeling steps come in to clean up the results.
> 
> The real question is whether the signal actually exists where it matters. On the stepped_block fine case, the critical test is those narrow 12-face strips between fillets — the interior faces are only 1-2 hops from junction faces, so the distance transform won't create much separation in absolute terms. But that's actually okay, because what matters is finding the local maxima relative to each surface, not absolute distance. The seed just needs to land somewhere on the surface itself, and the local maxima of the diffused field should naturally identify the interior-most face of each strip.
> 
> However, there's a real problem with the jet signal on these flat regions: the dihedral angles between adjacent flat faces are only 1.24°, so the jet isn't actually capturing the true planes. The Delaunay grading creates sliver rings inside the planes, and since the jet is computed from vertex neighborhoods, it becomes noisy on these slivers. This means the edge-strength field will have spurious ridges scattered throughout the planes themselves — diffusion helps smooth some of this noise, but the underlying issue remains that the local signals are unreliable on this model.
> 
> The sliver rings are a real geometric structure, not zero-mean noise, so diffusion alone won't eliminate them completely. On coarser meshes, the problem gets worse: dihedrals can reach 24° per row and the jet becomes essentially garbage, with curvature spreading 100× across a single arc. Using edge strength from jet mismatch would create massive noise, so I'd need to rely on raw dihedral second differences instead — but on medium meshes, a fillet only spans 2-4 rows, which barely gives enough samples to assume constant dihedral within the surface.

> At coarse resolution, the signal-to-noise ratio is fundamentally poor for any local detector, which is why detection got deprioritized in the first place. The seam assumption matters here too: boundaries are mesh-edge chains, so edge strength should really live on edges themselves, not faces — measuring how well the two adjacent faces' jets agree across each edge. Sorting ascending by the diffused edge strength roughly achieves "interior-most first" globally, though the local-maxima approach matters if you want finer control over seeding per basin.

> The practical improvement is straightforward: replace the raw 1-ring mismatch with a diffused version by running a few rounds of neighbor averaging on the mismatch field (around 5-10 iterations), which is O(k·N), then sort ascending as before.

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdX5CRZgfjrYzbczDMuUm -->

Yes — there's a direct facet-space analogue, and it's worth building. But the record forces one reinterpretation up front, and rewards care in choosing the "gradient" signal. Here's how I'd map it.

## The crucial reframe: ordering, not detection

Your pipeline (gradient → blur → maxima) is, in image terms, a soft distance transform: watershed seeding from regional maxima. The measured graveyard here is specific about what's dead: **curvature-threshold seam *detection*** — "no threshold separates real junctions from real curvature gradients, the classes overlap" (2026-07-20 pivot). But your proposal doesn't need a threshold anywhere, and that's exactly what saves it. A diffused evidence field used to *rank* seeds is a prior, not a gate — the jet's own demotion, applied again. If a maximum is wrong, visited≠claimed prices it at one wasted turn. So the line to hold in the design: the field orders; validation still decides everything. The moment it becomes a binary "this is/isn't a junction," it re-enters the measured dead end.

Notably, the skeleton already exists: seeds are sorted ascending by [neighbor_jet_mismatch](src/stage2/compete.rs:1205) — a raw, 1-ring, undiffused edge-strength field. Your idea is an upgrade of that exact slot, not new architecture.

## The mapping

- **Gradient** → per-edge junction evidence on the dual graph (faces = pixels, adjacencies = the 4-neighborhood). More below on what the evidence should be.
- **Blur** → k rounds of neighbor averaging of that field over faces (graph heat diffusion; k ≈ 5–10 covers the scales here, O(k·N) ≈ microseconds on 26 k faces). Crucially, treat the region's **sharp perimeter as a source too** — competition runs inside a smooth region, and its known boundary is maximal evidence — so maxima end up far from both sharp *and* tangent edges.
- **Maxima** → seed order by ascending diffused field. You don't even need explicit basin-maxima extraction at first: the existing global sort over the smoothed field puts basin interiors first, and once a surface commits, `interior_of_claims` skips the rest of its basin anyway. Use only the *maxima* side of watershed; deliberately do **not** use its basins as boundaries — boundary placement is the pipeline's proven machinery (flood, `resolve_claims`, relabel — zero wrong-side faces corpus-wide), and it's also watershed's classic weakness.

## The signal: not the jet — exact normals, in millimeters

This is where the measured record bites hardest. The jet is the wrong evidence source: adjacent jet-"flat" faces on stepped_block have a **median dihedral of 1.24°** (the jet's flat set is not the planes), and on coarse meshes it reads κ₁ = 0.023/0.162/2.2 across one exact arc — diffusing garbage yields smoothed garbage.

What behaves is exact facet normals, and there's a signal the TODO's partially-explored normals item already asked for without naming it ("the separation is between *constant* dihedral and *zero* dihedral… wants a different statistic"): the **dihedral second difference**. Within a tessellated surface, the dihedral field is locally consistent — ~0 across a plane, a steady θ per row across a fillet. At a tangent junction it *steps*: 0 → θ/2 → θ. So per edge, compare its dihedral against the "continuation" edges (the most-parallel edges of the two adjacent faces — well-defined on this corpus's fused quads, fuzzier on triangles); the discrepancy is the evidence. Two refinements: measure it in **mm of sag, not radians** (w·δ/4 — deviation from the common plane), so it's scale-free across tessellation densities and can be read in quanta per B-prime thinking; and compare like directions with like — on 155:1 strips, along-strip edges carry ~0 and cross-strip edges carry θ, and mixing them manufactures false ridges.

## Why this survives the narrow-strip geometry

The objection that killed the blind patch — 12-face planes where *everything* is near a junction — doesn't kill this, because diffusion maxima are **relative, not absolute**. A 12-face strip still has an interior-most face, and it's a local maximum of its own little basin regardless of being only one hop from the junction. That's precisely what "blur then find maxima" buys over any fixed-radius notion of "far from edges."

## What it buys — and what it honestly doesn't

**It does not fix today's blind patch.** The record is blunt: zero of 200 sampled flat seeds produced a clean patch *regardless of seed position* — patch construction was the lever, not placement. Against the current pipeline this field is a mild efficiency prior. Its real value is contingent on the bridge design from our last exchange, where seed position suddenly matters a lot: an interior seed's first accretions stay on-surface, so bridges start uncontaminated and identify before they ever meet a junction. The field also earns a **second job inside the bridge: junction-last accretion** — accrete low-evidence frontier faces first, deferring junction-adjacent ones to when the fit is best-conditioned to judge them. That's the principled replacement for the ordering role the jet currently plays.

There's also a direct runtime story for TODO item 2: ordering doesn't shrink the 26122-seed list, but earlier correct commits cascade through `interior_of_claims` — every surface committed early converts its whole interior into skipped seeds. Ordering quality is effectively the knob on how many seeds do real work, which is item 2's question verbatim.

**Graceful failures**, all costing turns rather than correctness: sliver rings inside planes (real Delaunay-grading structure, not zero-mean noise) may split a plane into two basins — an extra seed; OCCT Delaunay interiors have noisy per-edge dihedrals — diffusion is the antidote, but expect a weaker field there (the recorded provenance caveat); genuinely signal-free junctions (large-radius fillets whose per-row step is sub-noise) get no help — that's the measured-hard strip-vs-B-spline residue, unreachable by any local signal; and an OpenSCAD constant-dihedral prism yields a featureless field — arbitrary order, i.e., today's behavior.

## Suggested shape of the experiment

~40 lines, orthogonal to the bridge spike and worth doing first since it's cheaper: compute per-edge sag-discrepancy evidence, diffuse k rounds with the sharp perimeter as source, and swap the result into the seed-sort slot in [compete_region](src/stage2/compete.rs:244) (keep `neighbor_jet_mismatch` behind a flag for A/B). Measure exactly three things: NO-GROWTH count (baseline 323 on fine), non-skipped-seed count, and wall time, plus the usual `tmp/probe.sh` breakdowns to confirm decompositions are bit-identical or better. If the field is good, the win shows up as fewer effective seeds at identical output — and you'll have validated the evidence signal the bridge design wants to inherit before committing to the larger build.

I've recorded both ideas (bridge + diffused ordering field, marked proposed-not-built) in memory so the design thread survives into the next session.
