## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdX6DBwXpr5kU4RqRuiyk -->

Here's the plan. I've weighed everything from this session on its merits — including demoting some of what we discussed (the subdivision search doesn't make the cut; the diffusion field is an optional sub-step, not a phase; the bridge design earns its place but behind a validation gate). The organizing judgment: **brepper's fragility is not a tuning problem, it's three structural debts** — evaluation is anecdotal (14 hand-watched models from mostly one exporter), two tolerances are guessed rather than measured, and one component (the blind seed patch) forces every downstream guard to exist. The plan retires those debts in dependency order, with every phase gated on measurements a weaker executor can run mechanically.

---

# Robustness-first rework plan

**Goal.** Maximum robustness over all plausible CAD-derived meshes; minimum fragility (fewer tuned constants, no cliffs). Not goals: corpus score for its own sake, incrementality, preserving today's exact output.

**Standing principles** (the executor must not violate these; they're the distillation of this project's measured history):
1. Validation decides geometry; priors only order and prune.
2. Never add or retune a threshold to fix one model — find the mechanism. If a new tuned constant seems necessary, **stop and ask the user first**.
3. Before designing anything, read the dead-end lists (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN: "the blind patch is the root cause", "Identifiability is not model selection", "Mesh provenance"; TODO's island-candidacy and MDL post-mortems). Re-deriving a recorded dead end is the primary failure mode of this project.
4. Every change is judged by: `tmp/probe.sh` per-type breakdowns (counts alone hid a 3-torus regression), the full suite (`cargo test --release --no-fail-fast`), and — once Phase 0 lands — the harness. Record dead ends in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN before abandoning them.
5. A model with only stage-1 coverage is a model nobody is watching. Anything built gets end-to-end `--compare` coverage or `#[ignore]` with a blocker string.

---

## Phase 0 — Make robustness measurable (prerequisite for everything)

You cannot optimize generality against 14 memorized models. This phase builds the instrument; **no behavioral changes allowed**.

- **0a. Close coverage holes.** Full-pipeline `--compare` tests for `nosecone_truncated` and any other model found with stage-1-only coverage (grep the tests; the TODO says "go look for more"). `#[ignore]` with blockers where they fail.
- **0b. Generalization harness.** A script that: generates N randomized variants via `gen_test_models` (radii, feature sizes, tessellation densities, coarse/medium/fine), runs the pipeline, auto-scores against the generated reference STEPs (surface-count exactness, per-type breakdown, stage 3.1 edge distance, stage 4.1 volume, runtime), and diffs against a saved baseline. One command, machine-readable output.
- **0c. Provenance diversification.** The corpus is Onshape/Parasolid-heavy. We hold reference STEPs — so mesh them *ourselves* with OCCT at several deflections and feed those meshes to the pipeline, scored against the same STEPs. This gives a second faceter (constrained Delaunay) with perfect ground truth for free. Also generate the off-origin variants TODO B-prime specifies (same part at origin and ~1 m out). **Assumption test:** OCCT meshing is reachable through the existing bindings; if not, stop and ask.
- **0d. Fragility meter.** Automate the ablation protocol: for each stage-2 constant, perturb ±2× (or disable), run the harness, record which models change. Headline metrics from here on: **constant count** (34 today) and **cliff count** (constants whose perturbation collapses any model). Fragility is now a number, not an anecdote.

**Exit:** harness + baselines committed; dashboard runs in one command. **Stop and ask** if `gen_test_models` can't produce enough variety to distinguish models (i.e., harness scores are saturated/identical across variants).

## Phase 1 — Derive the tolerances from the mesh (kills guessed constants)

Two of three tolerances are stand-ins for discarded exporter settings; both are measurable from the mesh itself. This is low-risk, high-generality work, fully specified in TODO items B-prime/B — follow those write-ups, they contain the estimator math and the validation protocol.

- **1a. Quantum.** Compute the float32 precision floor per mesh; clamp `--vertex-tolerance` to it, warn, and print it in the stage-1 summary. Report fit quality in quantum units in diagnostics. Do **not** build the per-point floor unless the off-origin model from 0c shows it binds (it's measured inert on today's corpus — test the assumption before building).
- **1b. Surface tolerance from sag.** Implement both estimators (fit-based from unambiguous regions; fit-free `dihedral × facet size / 8`) and validate against the true sag that stage-1 `--compare` already reports (8.9e-4 / 9.5e-4 mm on the two measured models, vs the 0.4 default — ~400× off). Reference STEPs are the validation instrument, never a production input. Replace the default with the estimate (high quantile + safety margin, never a median) only after a full harness sweep — `REFIT_GIVE_UP`, merge gates, and every `face_validates` inherit this change. **Stop and ask** if the two estimators disagree by >5× on any corpus model (either the estimator is wrong or the provenance premise is violated — both are findings).
- **1c. Angular tolerance from the dihedral distribution** (Otsu-style gap). Validate against Onshape's known 12.5°/6.25°/2.5° settings. **Stop and ask** if no clean gap exists on more than a couple of models.
- **1d. Provenance-violation detector.** Estimator disagreement (1b) and gap absence (1c) become a stage-1 warning: "this mesh appears decimated/remeshed/faceted-by-construction." Wire the warning now; graceful-degrade behavior comes in Phase 5.

**Exit:** defaults computed from the mesh corpus-and-harness-wide with no regressions; flags remain as user overrides.

## Phase 2 — Replace the blind seed patch with validated incremental growth

The blind patch is the measured root cause of the largest fragility cluster (the `PATCH_*` family and the cliffs around it). The replacement is the **flood–stall–bridge** design (recorded in memory `seeding-bridge-design`): plane floods exact from one facet; when it stalls, richer types *bridge* — accrete face-by-face from the validated support plus stall frontier, refitting and revalidating the whole support each step — until their new DOF are **identifiable** (sweep for cylinders/tori, `axis_normals_consistent` for cones, a new normal-spread test for spheres), then flood to extent. Commit gates, area selection, `resolve_claims` unchanged. `promote_bend` is the existing proof of concept.

Build order, each step A/B-measured on the harness behind a flag:
1. Plane-floods-from-seed (expect the 323 flat-seed NO-GROWTH events on stepped_block fine → near zero; **stop and ask** if not, the design's premise is wrong).
2. Cylinder bridge. **Assumption tests:** promotion gate refuses the wall-plus-fillet-row impostors (count sweep rejections); coarse meshes identify within ~3 rows. Validation targets are `stepped_block_toroidal` **medium** and `part_rounded_cube` **coarse** — the recorded one place the blind patch is irreplaceable. **Stop and ask** if either regresses versus the blind patch.
3. Sphere normal-spread identifiability (also closes the vacuous `candidate_sweep` for spheres/cones).
4. Torus: measure whether a bridge beats keeping `promote_bend` + (d2) as-is; keep whichever measures better.
5. Only when the harness shows bridge ≥ blind patch everywhere: delete `seed_patch`/`condition_patch`/`grow_patch` and the five `PATCH_*` constants (TODO step 5's list).
6. Optional, evidence-gated: the diffused junction-evidence seed/accretion ordering — build only if NO-GROWTH counts or runtime say ordering is still a limiter.

**Exit:** blind patch deleted; harness ≥ baseline; runtime ≤ baseline on the 26k-face model (the 42→103 s regression should partially revert since attempts die at 2–3 faces instead of after 32-face conditioning).

## Phase 3 — Make freeform first-class (fit, then selection)

Real-world models mix freeform and analytic; this is where "all plausible models" is currently weakest.

- **3a. Fix the freeform fitter first** (TODO's own instruction): `bspline_blister`/`bspline_saddle` fail the §2.7 pole-bound check and nothing recovers. This is a conditioning problem with pinned reproducing tests — diagnose the parameterization/knot placement, don't loosen the check.
- **3b. Analytic-vs-freeform selection — treat as an experiment, not a feature.** Five formulations are dead; **count- and fraction-based rules are forbidden**. Order of work: (i) measure the untried relative-area separation corpus+harness-wide (junk torus = 0.095% of its patch vs real cones at large fractions) before implementing anything; (ii) re-derive region-level MDL for the dozens-vs-six DOF case only (the refutation was for 1–2 DOF gaps and explicitly doesn't transfer); (iii) **stop and show the user the measurements before adopting any rule.** Success looks like: `hill_with_hole` at exactly 7, and `cone_tangent_planes` passing for a *reason* (pin the current accidental pass with a test that would catch the freeform fit swallowing a real cone).

## Phase 4 — Harvest: delete guards, then decide the endgame

With seams constrained (locus), tolerances measured, and seeding validated, most remaining guards should be inert. Run the existing four-step ablation protocol over the **harness** (not just the corpus), one constant at a time, in TODO's most-likely-dead-first order. Report constant count and cliff count in every commit that moves them.

Then the structural decision point: **if the cliff count is near zero, stop — VSA (item C) is unnecessary and its rewrite risk is unjustified.** If cliffs persist in the flood/claims machinery, present the evidence to the user and ask before starting VSA; it subsumes known-working code and is the one genuinely risky rewrite in this plan. Fold the pending `snap_boundaries` keep/delete decision into this gate (it's C's per-face criterion; its fate follows C's).

## Phase 5 — Real-world hardening and graceful degradation

- Run 3DBenchy and a handful of downloaded real-world STLs (mechanical and organic) end to end. Expected: provenance violations. Required behavior: the Phase-1 detector fires and the pipeline **degrades deliberately** — clear report of what the data supports, best-effort freeform/per-facet output, never a crash, never silent garbage. Define and test that mode.
- Runtime scaling measurement (100k–1M faces); set a budget; the bridge's incremental cost model should be measured, not assumed.
- OpenSCAD/faceted-by-construction: generate two corpus models, then implement the policy as an explicit reported decision (flag or auto-detect-and-announce), per the recorded analysis that the ambiguity is genuine and unmeasurable.
- Backlog after that: OBJ/PLY input, crack stitching, IGES.

---

**Consolidated stop-and-ask triggers** (besides the per-phase ones): any new tuned threshold; any harness regression without a mechanism; any urge to re-derive something in a dead-end list; deleting code that a phase gate hasn't cleared; anything touching stage-3/4 geometry math (out of scope for this plan).

The sequencing logic, in one line each: Phase 0 because you can't claim generality you can't measure; Phase 1 because every later A/B must run under the final tolerance regime; Phase 2 because the blind patch forces the guards Phase 4 wants to delete; Phase 3 because selection needs a working freeform hypothesis to select against; Phase 4 last because deletion is only safe once the causes are gone; Phase 5 because honest failure on out-of-scope input is itself a robustness feature.

If this looks right, I'll write it to a file (and reconcile it with TODO.md so there's one source of truth).

77	To my mind the biggest conundrum is seeding - single-face seeds aren't working well and it's unclear why - perhaps because we're just exploring boundary faces and good seeds require an interior face. However, a brute-force search of all faces for seeds is computationally expensive. This is effectively a search problem. Doing a random search or a recursive subdivision search could result in finding good seeds much faster, but doesn't improve the worst-case when a nurbs surface is required. However, given that all vertices should lie precisely on the surface, it should be possible to fit surfaces using a decimated set of vertices. I'm wondering if some kind of recursive subdivision could identify surfaces quickly and bound the worst case? I'm imagining something like: for a given set of contiquous faces to try to fit a surface to: try to fit a surface to a subset of the vertices that includes some vertices at the corners and the interior of the patch; if there's a fit then retry with all vertices in the patch and if there's a perfect fit select the surface; if there's no fit but it's plausible there's a fit within then subdivide the patch and retry on the sub-patches; if there's clearly no fit then give up. I'm not sure how to distinguish between the two latter cases reliably. Read the code for surface fitting and stage 2, and think about whether my algorithm idea might have merit.

159	## Assistant
160	
161	I've read the seeding loop (\`compete.rs\`), the ladder (\`classify.rs\`), the \`SurfaceType\` fitters, the merge machinery, and confirmed nothing like recursive subdivision appears in the refuted-ideas list. Here's my assessment.
162	
163	## Short verdict: yes, it has real merit — and it's less foreign to the codebase than it sounds
164	
165	Your idea is best understood as: **make the existing ladder recursive instead of falling back to per-face seeding**. The root of your recursion already exists. \`fit_validate\` (classify.rs:371) does precisely your steps 1–2: fit on a stride subsample of vertices (capped at 3000 faces), validate against *all* vertices, and on a near-miss fold the worst offenders into the fit set and refit (\`REFIT_ADD\`/\`MAX_REFIT\`). What's missing is your step 3: today, when that fails, \`classify_regions\` gives up and hands the region to \`compete_region\`, which re-attempts "fit a small patch → validate by flooding" once per face. That's the 103 s. Your proposal replaces that brute-force fallback with recursion.
166	
167	## One correction to the diagnosis, from the code
168	
169	"Good seeds require an interior face" is right, but the *why* is largely measured already — it's in the TODO's step-5 notes:
170	
171	- Seeds aren't single faces today; each grows a blind ≤32-face BFS patch (\`seed_patch\`). Setting \`SEED_PATCH_CAP=1\` was measured: stepped_block fine collapses to 24,872 per-facet surfaces. A single facet pins a plane (3 DOF) but not a free 5-DOF cylinder fit — so the blind neighbourhood exists to bootstrap *parameters*, and it fails because its contamination lives in the fit parameters even though the flood re-validates faces.
172	- The seed *order* is already jet-interior-first (\`neighbor_jet_mismatch\` ascending), and the interior-skip means that once good surfaces commit, the faces left seeding are *exactly the junction-adjacent margins* — the worst possible seeds, whose patches straddle junctions. That's the "323 NO GROWTH seeds" and much of the runtime.
173	
174	So the failure isn't that interior seeds are unexplored — it's that the scheme must guess where the interior is, per face, with a blind patch. Your recursion never has to guess: **the hierarchy finds homogeneous supports by construction**. A node straddling a junction fails validation and splits; a node inside one surface validates. That's the step-5 blocker ("a bootstrap that is not a blind neighbourhood") dissolved from a different direction — you never seed at all.
175	
176	## Where the merit is concrete
177	
178	1. **Asymptotics.** Today: O(N) seeds × ~4 candidate types × up to 8 grow rounds × (LM fit + O(region) flood). The interior-skip is a heuristic patch on exactly this. The tree: depth ⌈log₂(N/size-floor)⌉ (~10 for the 26K-face model), validation O(N) closed-form distance evals per level, one subsampled fit per node. Rough estimate puts the whole decomposition of stepped_block fine near ~1 s vs 103 s. This is the principled answer to TODO item 2's question ("how much of the seed sweep does useful work"): only nodes on root-to-leaf paths that contain boundaries matter, and the tree visits exactly those.
179	2. **Constant deletion.** \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\`, \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`, \`PATCH_RADII\`, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\`, \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`, seed ordering, interior-skip — the whole blind-patch family that step 5 wants gone and measured it can't yet delete. Also most of the (d)/(d2) rescue machinery: their entire job is manufacturing a clean support for the fitter, and tree nodes are clean supports.
180	3. **Simplification of competition semantics.** Largest-validated-area competition exists because seeds produce *overlapping* supports. Tree nodes are disjoint supports, so per-node simplest-DOF-first ladder order suffices — which also structurally prevents the degenerate-super-model problem (a free-R torus never out-areas a cylinder, because on a cylinder node the conical rung commits first and the torus rung is never reached).
181	4. **Bounded worst case**, as you said: pure-NURBS regions bottom out at per-facet planes (same terminal fallback as today), cost bounded by the tree size.
182	
183	## Your crux question: subdivide vs. give up
184	
185	I'd argue the distinction mostly **dissolves** if you set the policy up right. Two observations:
186	
187	- **Subdividing too often only costs time** (bounded, logarithmic). Committing to freeform too early costs *correctness* — the TODO has measured twice that a B-spline validates happily over genuinely-analytic supports ("the freeform patch validates over these faces is not evidence the region is freeform"). The asymmetry says: default to subdividing; give up only at the size floor, or into the freeform tail.
188	- **Don't gate the split decision on residual magnitude.** The project's history is littered with non-monotonic residual-threshold responses (the \`acceptance_tol\` removal, the facet-scaled-growth refutation). \`REFIT_GIVE_UP\` survives only as a give-up-on-augmenting test, and under recursion its role is just "fall through to the next rung, then split".
189	
190	So: per node, run the DOF ladder (jet-ordered, as now). If a rung validates → commit. Else split. At the size floor → per-facet plane or the freeform handoff. The one prune worth having for the NURBS worst case is *jet homogeneity*: a node whose curvature field is smooth and generic-bi-curved, where every analytic rung has failed, is a freeform candidate — stop descending and hand the whole subtree to 2.3(e). That's "jet as prior, not gate", consistent with the codebase's philosophy, and its unreliability on coarse meshes costs only time.
191	
192	## The three wrinkles your sketch doesn't cover (the honest additions)
193	
194	1. **Split needs its merge half.** Real surfaces get diced across sibling leaves (a 12-face plane spanning two nodes; a fillet strip across five). This is classic split-and-merge: adjacent leaves whose *union* validates as one freshly-fit surface merge, bottom-up. The merge test already exists — it's exactly \`merge_regions\`' "refit the union and revalidate" (repair.rs:459+). Note this also handles over-splitting within one surface for free.
195	2. **Identifiability inverts.** Today the flood gives global support first and \`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\` checks it after. In the tree, a sub-node of a real torus legitimately sweeps 5° and fits a garbage-R torus that *validates locally*. So per-node, the sweep guards must become "defer, don't reject": small-sweep fits stay uncommitted merge candidates; identifiability is checked at merge/commit time, when the union's sweep is large. This is the main conceptual rework — validation semantics unchanged, but "reject" becomes "not yet".
196	3. **Junction-row ownership still needs a terminal pass.** Leaves straddling a tangent junction bottom out at one facet (= a plane), which is the medium-model's "~14 facets survive as per-facet planes" failure mode. Keep \`resolve_claims\`' logic as a final absorption: leftover leaves join a neighbouring committed surface they validate on (DOF-first preference), else per-facet plane. This is also where item A's locus machinery plugs in for boundary precision — subdivision doesn't fix the 4-surplus-surfaces problem on its own.
197	
198	And two things to hold onto from the current design: children must be **mesh-contiguous** (the seam assumption — boundaries must remain edge chains; a spatial octree would violate this, a graph bisection preserves it), and the **B-spline stays out of the internal-node ladder** (last resort only, at pruned subtrees or the 2.3(e) tail).
199	
200	## What it does *not* solve
201	
202	- The island/model-selection problem (hill_with_hole's junk torus, strip-vs-B-spline). Subdivision arguably creates *more* locally-analytic-looking freeform fragments. Inconsistent-parameter fragments won't merge, which is weak evidence against them, but rejection still needs the model selection the TODO calls genuinely hard. Unchanged.
203	- \`bspline_blister\`/\`saddle\` (the freeform fit itself failing) — orthogonal.
204	- The NURBS worst case is *bounded*, but fast only if the jet-homogeneity prune fires.
205	
206	## Relation to item C (VSA)
207	
208	This is the divisive dual of VSA's agglomerative Lloyd iteration — same "alternate fit/assignment to a fixpoint" core, top-down instead of bottom-up. It's a *smaller* change than C: it replaces \`compete_region\`'s seed loop and keeps the ladder, \`fit_validate\`, \`resolve_claims\`, and the freeform tail intact, whereas C rewrites 2.3(b)–2.4 wholesale. If it works, it delivers a large fraction of C's promised parameter deletion; C could still follow for boundary refinement, or the tree could seed its initial proxies.
209	
210	## Suggested smallest experiment
211	
212	Per the project's own methodology: (1) profile the 103 s first (fits vs. floods vs. seed count) so the win is attributable; (2) build the recursion as a drop-in replacement for the seed sweep only — double-sweep-BFS bisection, ladder per node on boundary-inclusive subsamples, union-validate merge, terminal absorption — behind the same probe harness; (3) \`tmp/probe.sh\` + full suite, with stepped_block **medium** and part_rounded_cube **coarse** as the identifiability stress tests (the step-5 breadcrumbs' own advice). Constant count before/after as the headline metric.
213	
214	The one-sentence version: you've independently proposed split-and-merge segmentation, and this codebase is unusually well-prepared for it — the ladder, union-revalidation, claim resolution, and the freeform tail are all already built and tested; what's being replaced is only the part (per-face seeding) that every document in the repo already identifies as the problem.
215

220	It seems like blind seeding is a source of a lot of the seeding problems. Think about ways to grow seeds that fit the tolerance constraints without crossing surface boundaries. One idea is to start with one face and grow, promoting the surface type as needed - a single face fits a plane, two faces could fit a cylinder, three faces could fit a sphere or torus, etc.

223	
224	## Assistant
225	
226	This idea is well-aimed — it attacks the blind patch directly, which the TODO's step-5 notes identify as the root cause and the thing they can't yet delete. Let me think it through against the actual machinery, because there are some non-obvious facts in this codebase that make your proposal *stronger* than you state it, and a couple of hard cases worth naming.
227	
228	## Why the arithmetic is better than "one face = plane, two = cylinder"
229	
230	Facet **normals** are exact in this pipeline (facets are exactly planar; the jet is what's unreliable — normals aren't the jet). And there's a lovely geometric fact the codebase already half-knows: the chord of a circular arc has its normal pointing exactly radially at the arc midpoint. So for a cylinder tessellated in arc-step quads, **every facet normal is an exact radial direction**, and for two facets at different arc positions:
231	
232	**axis = n₁ × n₂** — exact, regardless of how coarse the arc steps are.
233	
234	Then the circle fit in the perpendicular plane uses *vertices*, which lie on the true surface to ~1e-6 (your earlier point, and the B-prime measurements). Compare the measured alternative in the TODO breadcrumbs: the jet reads κ₁ = 0.023 / 0.162 / 2.2 across one exact r=0.5 arc on medium — the jet is worthless there, but n₁×n₂ is exact at any tessellation density. This is the "cheap, DOF-reducing bootstrap" the step-5 breadcrumbs were hunting for, and it's self-derived rather than borrowed — the per-seed borrowed-axis fragmentation failure (75→100 surfaces, measured twice) doesn't apply, because the axis comes from the patch's own validated members.
235	
236	The same normal-line reasoning gives closed-form initializers up the ladder:
237	
238	- **Sphere, 2 faces**: normal lines concur at the center (nearly — skew by chordal sag; take the common-perpendicular midpoint).
239	- **Torus, 4 faces** — and this is the one the TODO calls "the open case, 7 DOF with no equally cheap constraint": a torus normal line lies in the meridian plane, i.e. *the normal line and the axis are coplanar*. Two lines (p,d) are coplanar iff (p₂−p₁)·(d₁×d₂) = 0; each facet gives one such equation in the axis (4 DOF: direction 2 + position 2), so four faces determine the axis in closed form, then R and r fall out of the normal-line geometry. That initializer didn't exist when the breadcrumbs were written, as far as I can see in the tree.
240	
241	## How it maps onto the code
242	
243	The change is smaller than the subdivision idea and slots into one place: replace \`seed_patch\` + \`condition_patch\` (compete.rs:1027–1180) with an **earn-your-patch** grow:
244	
245	1. BFS from the seed; maintain current type + fit (closed-form only — no LM during patch construction).
246	2. Per frontier face: does it validate against the current fit at vtol (the existing \`face_validates\`)?
247	   - Yes → admit, refit cheaply.
248	   - No → try **promotion**: refit the accepted set + candidate at the next type; if *all* accepted faces validate under the promoted fit, promote and admit.
249	   - No type validates → the face is across a seam. Exclude it. That's the boundary found, not a fit failure.
250	3. Hand the patch + type + parameters to \`grow_from\` unchanged — first LM polish happens there, flood extends to true extent, \`resolve_claims\` arbitrates, exactly as today.
251	
252	The property you wanted is now structural: **every patch member was admitted by validation against a fit of already-admitted members**, so the patch can never straddle a junction. The blind patch's failure mode — contamination living on in the fit *parameters* — has no way to happen. And note what this does to seed quality: a margin seed's first ring includes faces across the junction, which fail admission and get excluded, so growth proceeds into the interior. The 323 NO GROWTH seeds mostly become productive; seed ordering (\`neighbor_jet_mismatch\`) stops mattering.
253	
254	**What retires:** \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\`, \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`, \`PATCH_RADII\`, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\`, \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`, all of \`condition_patch\` (the patch is clean by construction — no trimming needed), and probably \`MAX_TORUS_STARTS\` (multiple flood starts exist because the fit patch was unreliable). Each is on the TODO's "prize, quantified" list.
255	
256	## The hard cases, named honestly
257	
258	**1. Genuine ambiguity → branch, don't choose.** Two same-height faces of a cylinder have normal lines intersecting *on the axis* — so a 2-face patch validates as a sphere of radius R centered there (this is the hole→sphere trap at patch scale). The answer is to grow both branches; the false branch self-prunes within 1–2 faces, because the next face at a different height is off the sphere by ≈ Δ²/2R ≈ 6e-4 mm ≫ vtol. Validation at vtol is sharp relative to tessellation steps, so false candidates die young and branching stays cheap. Jet ordering (\`region_is_developable\`) can prioritize branches but must not gate — same philosophy as now.
259	
260	**2. The bent tube is the subtle one, and it needs a different promotion trigger.** Locally, a bend *is* a cylinder to within vtol (sag of the bend over a 2 mm patch at R=14 is ~3.6e-5 mm), so "validation failed" never fires and promotion to torus never triggers. Worse, the patch isn't straddling anything it shouldn't — it's legitimately one tube. The signal is **fit drift**: along a straight cylinder, successive refits are stationary; along a bend, the fitted axis precesses systematically as the support grows. Drift of the same-typed fit as support accumulates = wrong type; cylinder precessing → try the 4-face torus initializer on the accumulated patch. If this works, \`promote_bend\` (~130 lines, the \`tube_jet\` signature, \`BEND_PATCH_DEPTH\`/\`BEND_PATCH_CAP\`, the stall-frontier analysis) folds into the general promotion rule — 2.3(c) stops being a special case. This is the piece I'd prototype first, because it's the one with a genuinely new observable; everything else is assembly of existing parts.
261	
262	**3. What it deliberately does not change — which is a feature.** Commit semantics stay exactly as today: the committed face set is a flood output, global, all-validated. So \`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\` and the identifiability guards keep their meaning (no inversion problem like the subdivision scheme has), \`resolve_claims\` still owns junction rows, and the medium-model per-facet-sliver problem is neither fixed nor worsened.
263	
264	**4. What it does not fix.** The seed *sweep* cost (item 2): every margin face still seeds once — patches get cheaper and more reliable, but it's still O(N) seeds, and perversely, fewer NO GROWTH seeds means *more* successful grows, so this could raise runtime before the subdivision idea lowers it. And the model-selection problem (hill_with_hole's junk torus): incremental growth on a B-spline hill grows a small locally-valid cone exactly as today, because it *is* locally valid. That stays the known hard problem.
265	
266	## Positioning against the subdivision idea
267	
268	They compose rather than compete: incremental typed growth replaces the blind **patch** (the step-5 blocker, the thing that produces wrong fits); subdivision replaces the seed **sweep** (the asymptotic bound, item 2). I'd do this one first — it's a fraction of the size, preserves every guard's semantics, and if it works, the subdivision scheme's per-node fitting gets a bootstrap for free. The breadcrumbs in TODO step 5 (candidate axes: borrowed/jet/covariance/long-edge) get superseded by a cleaner answer: derive the axis from two validated faces' normals, per type, in closed form.
269	
270	## Smallest measurable experiment
271

281	## User
282	
283	One more thing to ponder: finding surface boundaries at tangent edges is a challenge. A goal is to find seads that are far from tangent edges and thus maximally likely to be fully contained in one surface. If this were an image processing problem, I'd think about it as an edge-finding & blurring problem: compute pixel gradients to find edges, then blur until the edge data is diffused a good distance from the edges, then look for local maxima to identify points far from edges. Can we do something similar in facet-space?
284	
285	---
286	
287	## Assistant
288	
289	Yes — and the facet-space version of your idea is a well-mapped piece of mathematics. But its value hinges entirely on one question: **what feeds the edge map**. Let me take the pieces in order.
290	
291	## The translation to facet-space is direct (and cheap)
292	
293	Your image-processing pipeline has exact named analogs on the dual graph (faces = nodes, adjacency = edges):
294	
295	- **"Blur the edge data and find points far from edges"** = the **distance transform** (grassfire transform). On a graph it's multi-source Dijkstra from edge faces, weighted by centroid gaps — which, note, is in *millimetres*, not hops. That matters here: \`PATCH_RADII\` exists precisely because hop counts lie on graded meshes (three hops walk ~18 mm along a fillet chain swept with 6 mm facets). A geodesic distance field has no such problem, and it's parameter-free — no blur radius, no iteration count, no λ.
296	- **"Local maxima far from edges"** = maxima of the distance field, i.e. the **medial axis** in facet-space. (Small sign correction to your description: if you blur the edge map itself, maxima sit *at* edges — you want maxima of distance-to-edge, equivalently minima of diffused edginess. Same object.)
297	- **"Seeds at the maxima"** = farthest-first / maximin seeding (Gonzalez's k-center traversal). It has a real guarantee: each successive seed maximizes distance from all boundaries and all previous seeds, so the sequence is provably spread — a much better answer than \`neighbor_jet_mismatch\`, which is a noisy jet-based *proxy* for exactly this quantity.
298	
299	So as a seed-ordering field: sound, standard, and a strict improvement on what \`compete_region\` does today.
300	
301	## The catch: the edge map is the hard part, and one source of it is measured dead
302	
303	Three possible sources, in order of trustworthiness:
304	
305	**1. Sharp edges — exact and free, but blind to the junctions that matter.** Stage 2.2 already detects sharp edges, and regions are connected components over non-sharp edges (\`regions.rs\`). A distance field from region boundaries is computable in one Dijkstra pass. But regions are *defined* as tangent-connected interiors — the tangent junctions you actually want distance from are invisible to this source. On stepped_block, one 26122-face region contains all 71 surfaces; distance-from-sharp-boundary tells you nothing about where the fillets are.
306	
307	**2. Curvature-based tangent-edge detection — measured dead, do not re-derive.** This is the 2026-07-20 pivot in TODO: no threshold separates real junctions from real curvature gradients. And there's a geometric reason it's worse than "noisy": at a tangent junction between a fillet and a plane, the fillet's arc steps *approach* the plane's slope, so the dihedral **dips** toward zero exactly at the seam. The junction is locally the *smoothest* place on the mesh — an anti-edge. Every local geometric field (jet, dihedral, curvature-step) inherits some version of this, which is why detection-as-gate kept failing.
308	
309	**3. Validation-failure frontiers — exact, and you already own the machine that produces them.** From the incremental-grow idea: when the earn-your-patch grow excludes a face, that face is across a seam. The excluded frontier *is* the tangent junction row, discovered as a side effect of fitting, with no threshold anywhere. This is the only tangent-edge map in the system that's exact.
310	
311	## The synthesis: distance field with feedback, not precomputation
312	
313	Putting those together, the right construction isn't "detect edges → distance transform → seed", it's a loop:
314	
315	1. Initialize distance sources = sharp boundaries (exact, free).
316	2. Seed at the **max-distance unexplained face** (farthest-first).
317	3. Grow with the earn-your-patch mechanism; commit the flood.
318	4. Mark the grow's *failure frontier* as new distance sources; update the field (incremental multi-source Dijkstra — cheap).
319	5. Repeat until nothing is unexplained.
320	
321	This replaces the jet-mismatch ordering with a geometric ordering, and every iteration's seed is maximally far from everything known to contaminate a fit — sharp edges, plus tangent seams discovered by previous grows. No detector, no threshold, no new tuned constants.
322	
323	**And it yields a principled answer to TODO item 2's actual question** ("how much of the 26122-face seed sweep does useful work"). The useful-work criterion: a seed can only claim new territory if its conditioning ball contains an *unclaimed* face. So maintain distance-to-nearest-unclaimed-face; skip any seed whose conditioning radius is smaller than that distance. That's a strict generalization of today's \`interior_of_claims\` (which checks only the radius-1 neighbourhood — compete.rs:420) to the true conditioning radius, and unlike a heuristic cap it's *sound*: a ball fully inside one committed surface can only refit that surface. On stepped_block fine, after the first few medial seeds commit the big surfaces, the unclaimed set collapses to junction zones, and the sweep collapses from 26K candidate seeds to the few hundred faces adjacent to leftovers. That's the runtime lever item 2 wants, derived rather than tuned.
324	
325	**Bonus use — identifiability.** A face's distance value caps the support its patch can gather, which caps the DOF its fit can determine: the degenerate free-radius tori (the ones \`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\` exists to reject) are born at faces whose reachable support is a handful of faces. Distance-from-boundary gives a per-seed "max supportable DOF" prior — it can cap the promotion ladder from the incremental-grow idea (don't promote to torus where the ball can only reach 7 faces), attacking the degeneracy at seed time rather than at commit time.
326	
327	## What to be honest about
328	
329	- **Idea 2 changed this idea's job.** Once patches are earned face-by-face under validation, seed interiority matters much less for *correctness* — a margin seed's first ring excludes the cross-junction faces and grows inward. What distance-seeding still buys is runtime (the skip above) and fit conditioning/identifiability (medial seeds gather sweep and area). Worth having, but it's no longer load-bearing for correctness.
330	- **Any field computed before fitting is a prior; the only exact field comes from fitting.** If someone proposes feeding this with a jet/dihedral edge map, that's the refuted detector returning in a hat.
331	- **The full watershed reading is a bigger, different bet.** If you treat a continuous edginess field as topography and let basins flood until they meet, the watershed lines *are* the partition — no seeds at all. That's a legitimate third architecture (and notice: watershed ridge lines are exactly the *optimal split boundaries* for the recursive-subdivision idea — all three of your ideas converge on the same object). But it inherits watershed's classic over-segmentation on freeform regions, and it presupposes a topography whose trustworthiness is precisely what's measured dead. I'd file it under "interesting, not yet".
332	
333	## Prior art worth mining
334	
335	Distance/grassfire transform (Borgefors), Blum's medial axis, marker-controlled watershed (Beucher & Vincent) — the marker version is the relevant one, and your "known-interior seeds" *are* the markers; farthest-first traversal (Gonzalez 1985); and your "blur then climb to maxima" is literally **mean-shift** mode-finding, whose mesh-segmentation cousins exist. Mesh-saliency literature also computes geodesic distance-to-boundary on dual graphs routinely.
336	
337	## Smallest experiment
338	
339	1. Compute geodesic distance-to-region-boundary per face (one multi-source Dijkstra per region, centroid-gap weights — maybe 30 lines).
340	2. Replace the \`neighbor_jet_mismatch\` sort with descending distance; add the distance-to-unclaimed skip in place of \`interior_of_claims\`.
341	3. \`tmp/probe.sh\` must stay bit-identical (ordering alone must not change results — if it does, that's a bug to understand); the suite is the decider; the 103 s on stepped_block fine is the metric to watch.
342	
343	One-sentence version: the distance-transform instinct is right and maps cleanly onto the dual graph, but in this problem the edge map can't come from a detector — it has to come from the fits themselves, so the build is a feedback loop (farthest-first seed → validated grow → failure frontier becomes new edge sources) rather than a precomputed blur.

355	# Rework plan: brepper toward structural robustness
356	
357	## The thesis in one paragraph
358	
359	The measured fragility of brepper concentrates in exactly one place: **stage 2.3(b)'s seeding**, and the pile of ~34 constants that exist because tangent seams are invisible to local geometry. Everything upstream (stage 1, sharp-edge detection → regions, the DOF ladder, \`fit_validate\`, the validation primitives) and everything downstream (claim arbitration, stage 3 assembly, the \`--compare\` harness) is principled and load-bearing. So this plan does **not** rewrite the pipeline. It replaces the blind-patch seeding mechanism with fits that are *earned under validation* rather than *grabbed and repaired*, makes seed exploration distance-guided so its cost scales with boundary-zone size rather than region size, deletes whatever is then measured inert on an expanded corpus, and quarantines the two genuine research problems (freeform fit conditioning; analytic-vs-B-spline model selection) behind explicit measurement gates. A partition-level rewrite (recursive subdivision / watershed) is held in reserve and only started with the user, with data, if the earlier phases leave the goals unmet.
360	
361	---
362	
363	## Execution rules (apply to every commit, every phase)
364	
365	These are the guardrails that make the plan followable. Violating one means stop.
366	
367	1. **Invariants that must hold after every commit:** every mesh face ends up in exactly one surface (\`assert_invariants\` exists); committed supports are flood/validation outputs at \`vtol\`/\`stol\` — never adjusted by hand; \`cargo clippy\` clean; build clean; the test suite green (or newly \`#[ignore]\`d only with a blocker string).
368	2. **Measurement protocol, unchanged from project convention:** save a \`tmp/probe.sh\` baseline before each phase; diff **per-type breakdowns** (plane/cyl/cone/sphere/torus/bspline), never surface counts alone; the full release suite (\`cargo test --release --no-fail-fast\`) is the decider. Report the stage-2 constant count (currently **34**) in every commit message.
369	3. **No new tolerance-like or threshold-like constants without explicit user sign-off.** Numeric-conditioning guards (determinant floors, minimum sin(dihedral) before normalizing a cross product) are not thresholds; say so in their doc comments when adding them.
370	4. **Nothing is deleted without ablation evidence** (disable → probe → suite → then delete, recording what superseded it).
371	5. **Stop and ask the user** — with measurements written down — when: any phase's listed assumption check fails; a result is *ordering-dependent* (ordering changes output = fragility, which is what we're removing); you're about to implement a freeform-candidacy or model-selection rule (five have failed already — no sixth lands unmeasured); a detector seems to need "just one threshold"; a guard proposed for deletion still fires anywhere on the corpus.
372	6. **Known dead ends — do not re-derive:** curvature/dihedral/seam *detection* (dead, twice; tangent junctions are locally the *smoothest* places on the mesh); acceptance-tolerance scaling with the float32 quantum (chaotic, no plateau); complexity pricing among nested analytic types (arithmetic doesn't reach the case); cluster-and-refit fitted radii (user-rejected); per-seed borrowed-axis commits (fragments surfaces, measured twice); face-size-ratio boundary tests (anti-correlated); retuning flood/conditioning floors (non-monotonic everywhere).
373	
374	---
375	
376	## Phase 0 — Make robustness measurable
377	
378	**Goal:** "robust over all plausible CAD models" must become a testable claim, not an aspiration. The corpus is Onshape/Parasolid-heavy and 14 models; several models were silently broken precisely because nothing watched them.
379	
380	**Build:**
381	- **An OCCT-provenance STL generator.** brepper links OCCT already; add a small \`bin/\` utility that loads a reference STEP and facets it with \`BRepMesh\` at several deflection/angular settings. This yields OCCT/Delaunay-provenance meshes of *existing ground truth* — provenance and density diversity nearly for free. Include off-origin variants (~1 m translation) so the float32-quantum question becomes testable.
382	- **Randomized sweep models** via \`gen_test_models\`/ccad: randomized radii, fillet chains, tangent cone chains, tori near r/R limits, thin walls, small features, multiple densities. Auto-score each against its generated reference: per-type surface breakdown, \`--compare\` distances, volume.
383	- **Coverage lock:** every corpus model gets at minimum a decomposition test; every model with a reference STEP gets a \`--compare\` test or an \`#[ignore]\` with a precise blocker. This is how three NURBS models rotted — close that hole permanently. (This picks up the existing \`nosecone_truncated\` item.)
384	- **Run 3DBenchy once**, record the diagnosis as a baseline entry in the sweep results. Don't fix anything found; just know it.
385	- **Quantum clamp:** compute the mesh's float32 quantum at stage 1; if \`--vertex-tolerance < quantum\`, clamp, warn, and print the quantum in the stage-1 summary. Tiny, safe, and fixes an unsatisfiable-request class of user error. (Using the quantum as a fit-quality *unit* is Phase 4, with its recorded DO-NOTs.)
386	
387	**Assumption check:** the harness reproduces today's known-good results on the existing 14 models. If not, the harness is wrong — fix it before anything else.
388	
389	**Stop and ask:** if OCCT-provenance meshes behave qualitatively differently (e.g., Delaunay interiors defeat assumptions the analytic path relies on), present the evidence and ask before adapting the pipeline — that's a provenance-policy decision.
390	
391	---
392	
393	## Phase 1 — Earned patches (the core rework)
394	
395	**Goal:** replace the blind seed patch with a patch *grown face-by-face under validation*, promoting surface type as accumulated DOF allow. A patch that can never straddle a junction by construction removes the root cause the step-5 notes document — the contamination that lives in fit *parameters*.
396	
397	**Build:**
398	1. **Closed-form initializers from exact facet normals** (LM is only for polish, in the existing \`grow_from\`):
399	   - Plane: one face (exact).
400	   - Cylinder: axis = n₁ × n₂ from two faces with non-parallel normals + \`fit_circle_for_axis\` (exists). Guard: only normalize the cross product above a numerical-conditioning floor; otherwise keep growing as plane until a better pair arrives.
401	   - Sphere: normal-line intersection from two faces (common-perpendicular midpoint).
402	   - Conical: existing normal-covariance machinery — sound now because the patch is clean.
403	   - Torus: derive the 4-face normal-line-coplanarity system (a torus's normal lines lie in the meridian planes, hence are coplanar with the axis). **Time-box this.** Unit-test on synthetic tori to ~1e-9 before integrating. If the derivation or conditioning doesn't pan out, *skip it* — direct torus seeding stays as today (\`torus_candidate\`), and Phase 1 is not blocked.
404	2. **The earn loop:** BFS from the seed; admit a frontier face iff it validates the current fit at \`vtol\`; on failure, attempt **promotion** in ladder order (plane → conical/sphere, jet-ordered as today → torus), refitting the accepted set + candidate and requiring *all* accepted faces to validate; if nothing validates, the face is across a seam — exclude it. **Branch** on genuine ambiguity (e.g., the 2-face cylinder/sphere trap): false branches should self-prune within a couple of faces because validation at \`vtol\` is sharp relative to tessellation steps.
405	3. **Cylinder→torus promotion via fit drift.** A bend is locally a valid cylinder, so validation never triggers promotion. The signal: along a straight cylinder successive refits are stationary; along a bend the fitted axis *precesses systematically*. Test drift as *sign-consistency* of successive axis deltas, not magnitude — if it needs a magnitude constant, **stop and ask**. Success here is what later allows deleting \`promote_bend\`; if it doesn't work, keep \`promote_bend\` and move on.
406	4. Hand patch + type + parameters to the **unchanged** \`grow_from\` flood/refit/commit/\`resolve_claims\` path. Commit semantics deliberately do not change: every guard (\`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`, torus-ratio, \`MIN_*_FACES\`) keeps its exact meaning.
407	
408	**Delete (after ablation):** \`seed_patch\` jet filtering, \`condition_patch\`, \`SEED_PATCH_CAP\`, \`MAX_PATCH_DEPTH\`, \`PATCH_RADII\`, \`PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS\`, \`MIN_CONDITION_FACES\`.
409	
410	**Assumption checks, in order, before building further:**
411	- *Axis-initializer quality across provenances.* On Parasolid quad strips, cylinder chord normals are exactly radial → n₁×n₂ exact. On OCCT Delaunay interiors this is *not* guaranteed. Measure axis error against reference cylinders across the Phase-0 corpus. If median error exceeds ~1° on some provenance, the initializer degrades to "covariance-axis over the earned patch" for that case — still clean, still not blind. **If even that underperforms the blind patch on any provenance, stop and ask** — the core premise needs re-examination.
412	- *Branch lifetime.* Instrument: branches should die within ~2 faces. If they persist, validation isn't as sharp as believed — stop and ask before the cost model changes.
413	- *NO GROWTH count* on the corpus should collapse toward zero.
414	
415	**Acceptance:** probe breakdowns equal-or-better on all models (stepped_block fine ≥ today's 75-surface state, medium not worse); suite green; constant count down by the deleted family.
416	
417	**Explicitly NOT fixed by this phase — do not chase them here:** the 4 surplus surfaces on fine (boundary precision), medium's gap, hill_with_hole's junk torus (model selection), runtime (Phase 2).
418	
419	---
420	
421	## Phase 2 — Seed exploration: ordering and bounded cost
422	
423	**Goal:** the 103 s question ("how much of the 26,122-face sweep does useful work") gets a *derived* answer: only seeds whose reachable ball contains an unclaimed face can do useful work.
424	
425	**Build, profile first:**
426	1. **Profile the runtime** (patch construction vs. LM fits vs. floods vs. claim resolution). Apply the cheapest fix that addresses the measured distribution; do not build the full machinery below if the profile says the cost is elsewhere. Report the profile to the user regardless.
427	2. **Distance field:** geodesic distance-to-region-boundary per face (multi-source Dijkstra, centroid-gap weights — millimetres, not hops; graded meshes make hop counts lie). Seed order: **farthest-first**, replacing \`neighbor_jet_mismatch\`.
428	3. **Duplicate abort:** a seed claimed by surface S whose minimal-patch ball lies entirely within S can only refit S — skip it (topological rule, strict generalization of \`interior_of_claims\`, no numeric tolerance).
429	4. **Frontier feedback (only if the profile says ordering still matters):** grow-failure frontiers (faces excluded by Phase-1 earns) become new distance sources for subsequent ordering. This is the exact tangent-edge map, discovered by fitting, never detected.
430	
431	**Acceptance:** runtime on stepped_block fine and the sweep substantially reduced; **results bit-identical or every diff explained** — ordering must not change output. If it does, that order-dependence is itself the fragility we're eliminating: stop and surface it.
432	
433	---
434	
435	## Phase 3 — Ablation and consolidation
436	
437	**Goal:** delete what Phases 1–2 made inert. Standard ablation protocol, one candidate at a time, sweep + suite, per-type breakdowns.
438	
439	**Candidates, in expected order:** \`promote_bend\` + \`BEND_*\` (if drift promotion works); \`rescue_with_neighbour_axes\` (d2) and possibly \`rescue_leftover_components\` (d) — earned patches may end the "never handed a clean face set" problem they exist for; \`MAX_TORUS_STARTS\`; \`MAX_GROW_ROUNDS\`; \`MERGE_MAX_SMALL\`; \`SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES\`; then the remaining degeneracy guards (\`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG\`, \`MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO\`) — *expect several to still fire; firing means keep, not tune.*
440	
441	**Also resolved here with data:** the pending \`snap_boundaries\` keep/delete question — re-run its ablation after Phases 1–2 and bring the numbers to the user for the call.
442	
443	---
444	
445	## Phase 4 — The two research problems (each gated by measurement)
446	
447	**4a. Freeform fit conditioning (\`bspline_blister\`, \`bspline_saddle\`).** A fitting problem with a clear signal: large components rejected by the pole-bound check with no recovery. Better-conditioned domains/resampling per the breadcrumbs already in bspline.rs. Acceptance: both models decompose; then the ignored invalid-wire tests re-examined.
448	
449	**4b. Quantum as the unit of fit quality** (B-prime). Committed analytics fit at 0.3–0.5 quanta; spurious fits at 80×. Build the per-point quantum and *report* fit quality in quanta as a diagnostic first. Honor the recorded DO-NOTs (no acceptance-tolerance scaling; direction-weighted, not |p|∞; least-squares averages quantization down ~√N). Any use as a *commit criterion* is a user decision with corpus measurements in hand.
450	
451	**4c. Analytic-vs-B-spline model selection** (\`hill_with_hole\` junk torus; strip-tessellated patch vs. piece of a larger B-spline). The one genuinely open problem. **Hard gate:** before implementing anything, measure the relative-area separation (junk torus = 0.095% of its patch's area; \`cone_tangent_planes\`' real cones are large fractions of theirs) across the *entire* sweep corpus, specifically re-checking \`cone_tangent_planes\` and the elliptical models, which killed the previous four formulations. Bring the measurements to the user; only then choose a mechanism. If the separation doesn't hold, stop — do not improvise a fifth rule.
452	
453	---
454	
455	## Phase 5 — Reserve: partition-level architecture (only with data + user sign-off)
456	
457	If Phases 1–3 leave sweep cost or seeding quality unacceptable *as measured on the Phase-0 corpus*, the remaining lever is replacing the per-face sweep with a partition: recursive subdivision (earned-patch fits at the leaves, union-validate merge bottom-up, identifiability guards becoming "defer, don't reject" until merge time), possibly using the distance field's watershed structure for split placement. This is the one massive rewrite on the table; it is deliberately last because Phases 1–2 deliver most of its practical benefits at a fraction of the risk. **Do not start without presenting Phase 1–3 measurements to the user.**
458	
459	---
460	
461	## What this plan deliberately does not touch
462	
463	Stage 1; sharp-edge detection and region extraction; the DOF ladder's structure and jet-informed rung ordering; \`fit_validate\`; \`face_validates\`; \`resolve_claims\` arbitration; stage 3; the \`--compare\` gates; the jet's role as prior/ordering device. These are the parts that already embody the right invariants — vertices are exact, validation is the arbiter, boundaries are mesh-edge chains. The rework's job is to make the one unprincipled component (seeding) obey the same invariants, then let deletion do its work.

> I'm looking at a parallel session that ran without extended thinking, and their approach to the earned-patch design differs from mine in some interesting ways. They've got a closed-form initializer strategy using exact facet normals that I hadn't considered — for instance, directly deriving the cylinder axis from the normal vector rather than my bridge approach.
> 
> The key insight they're exploring is whether the cross product of two facet normals gives you the axis exactly. For a tessellated cylinder, the facet normals are radial (perpendicular to the axis), so n₁ × n₂ should yield the axis precisely, regardless of how coarse the tessellation is. But this breaks down for cones, where normals make a constant angle with the axis rather than being perpendicular to it. And for real OCCT Delaunay triangles on a cylinder, the normals tilt slightly because vertices sit at different heights and angles, so the exact perpendicularity assumption doesn't hold anymore.
> 
> They're noting that the approach acknowledges this limitation: on Delaunay interiors, the exactness isn't guaranteed, so they measure the axis error and fall back to a covariance-based method if the error gets too large. For spheres, two normal lines nearly converge at the center, which works well. For tori, they're exploring whether normal lines are coplanar with the axis—but they're catching themselves: torus normals actually pass through the tube's spine circle, not the axis itself, and they're checking whether the normal still lies in the meridian plane. For grid-aligned quad facets, the chord normal naturally falls in the meridian plane of the facet center, making this a solid initializer even if approximate for other tessellations — they've built in a skip clause to handle cases that don't converge. The second phase uses an "earn-your-patch" loop where frontier faces that fail validation trigger promotion, and the promoted fit must validate all previously accepted faces; this differs from my bridge framing but serves the same gating purpose around identifiability.
> 
> On identifiability itself, they keep the sweep gate at commit time rather than gating promotion, whereas I'd gate promotion on whether new degrees of freedom are identifiable. With closed-form geometric initializers like the cross-product of normals for cylinder axes, promotion sidesteps the under-identification problem entirely — you get the axis exactly from the normal pair on strip meshes, then fit the radius with a well-conditioned circle fit on short arcs, avoiding the need to wait for many faces to resolve the bridge.
> 
> The sweep gate still catches degenerate cases at commit. For cylinders versus spheres on two faces, the ambiguity branches and false branches self-prune. Drift-triggered torus promotion works differently — a bend validates locally as a cylinder, so validation never triggers torus promotion; instead, systematic axis precession across refits signals the need to promote, with promote_bend as a fallback.
> 
> Their distance-field approach is cleaner than my diffused junction evidence sourced from dihedral second differences. They use sharp boundaries (exact, free) and validation-failure frontiers from previous grows (exact, no detector). They're right that my dihedral-derived edge map, even if ordering-only, is still detector-like and inferior to a fit-derived edge map that's exact and threshold-free — especially if you interleave fitting with seeding. Their construction uses farthest-first seeding on the distance field.
> 
> I also notice their "duplicate abort" heuristic, which skips seeds whose minimal-patch ball lies entirely within one committed surface — that's a topological generalization of interior_of_claims, derived rather than tuned. And their use of distance caps to bound supportable degrees of freedom as an identifiability prior is a nice touch too.
> 
> On recursive subdivision, they were initially more enthusiastic than I was, proposing split-and-merge with refit-union-validate and deferring identifiability checks rather than rejecting outright — that would've given ~1s vs 103s asymptotics. But they ultimately demoted it to Phase 5 reserve pending data and user sign-off, which converges with my own contingency verdict.
> 
> Looking at their phase structure versus mine, Phase 0 is quite similar — they're using OCCT to generate reference STEPs via meshing, randomized sweep models, coverage lock, and 3DBenchy baseline runs, with quantum clamp included early. > The key insight is that for an inscribed triangle on a sphere, the normal line anchored at the circumcenter (not the centroid) passes exactly through the sphere center — this works because the circumcenter is equidistant from all three vertices, so the line through it along the plane normal contains all points equidistant from the vertices, including the sphere center. The same exactness holds for planar quadrilaterals on a sphere since their vertices are concyclic. This is worth noting in the plan: anchoring normal lines at facet circumcenters gives exact concurrency for inscribed facets.
>

> The earn loop replaces the seed and condition patches inside grow by running a BFS that admits faces under validation, promoting them in ladder order only if all accepted faces validate under the promoted fit, branching on ambiguity, and recording excluded faces as frontier; instrumentation tracks no-growth counts, branch lifetimes, and frontier sizes.

## The Robustness Rework (2026-07-29)

**This section is the rationale behind the phased plan in TODO.md.** TODO.md
carries the step-by-step instructions; this section records why each phase is
shaped the way it is, the design detail for the two new mechanisms (earned
patches, distance-guided exploration), the corpus strategy, what was considered
and deliberately not adopted, and the measurements preserved from the TODO
items the plan retired. It synthesizes two parallel design sessions held
2026-07-29 (one logged at `agent-logs/2026-07-29_14-59_nothinking.md`) plus the
measured record above.

### Goal and thesis

The goal changed: **maximum robustness and minimum fragility over all plausible
CAD-derived meshes**, not score on the current corpus. Fragility is
operationalized as two headline numbers: the stage-2 tuned-constant count
(34 at plan time; `grep -h "^const \|^pub(crate) const \|^pub(super) const "
src/stage2/*.rs | wc -l`) and the **cliff count** — the number of constants
whose ±2× perturbation collapses any model (four unrelated knobs once each
flipped stepped_block medium from 83 surfaces to full per-facet collapse).

The measured fragility concentrates in three structural debts, and the plan
retires them in dependency order:

1. **Evaluation is anecdotal.** 14 hand-watched models, Onshape/Parasolid-heavy;
   three NURBS models rotted silently because nothing watched them. You cannot
   claim generality you cannot measure → Phase 0 builds the instrument first.
2. **Two of three tolerances are guesses.** `--surface-tolerance` defaults to
   0.4 mm against a measured true sag of ~9e-4 mm (~400× too loose), making
   centroid validation nearly vacuous; `--angular-tolerance` is a stand-in for
   a discarded exporter setting. Both are measurable from the mesh itself
   → Phases 0 (diagnostics) and 3 (regime flip).
3. **One component — blind-patch seeding — forces most of the guard pile.**
   `seed_patch`/`condition_patch` fit an unvalidated 32-face BFS neighbourhood;
   the contamination lives on in the fit parameters, producing both the 323
   NO-GROWTH seeds and plausible-but-wrong fits. Roughly two-thirds of stage
   2's constants exist because tangent seams are invisible to it → Phases 1–2
   replace the mechanism, Phase 4 deletes what that makes inert.

Everything else measured principled — stage 1, sharp-edge regions, the DOF
ladder, `fit_validate`, `face_validates`, flood, `resolve_claims`,
relabel+refit, the locus machinery, stage 3 topological assembly, the
`--compare` gates — **is deliberately not touched**.

### Phase 1 design: earned patches (replaces the blind patch)

Replace grab-then-repair with **grow-under-validation**: BFS from the seed,
admitting a frontier face only if it validates against the current fit at
vertex tolerance; when a face fails, attempt **promotion** up the ladder
(plane → cylinder/cone or sphere → torus), refitting accepted+candidate and
requiring *every already-accepted face* to still validate; if no type
validates, the face is across a seam — exclude it and record it as a
**frontier face** (Phase 2 consumes these). The patch can never straddle a
junction by construction, which is the exact property the blind patch lacks.
Commit semantics do not change: the earned (type, params, patch) goes to the
unchanged `grow_from` flood/refit path, and every existing commit gate
(`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG`, torus ratio, `MIN_*_FACES`, `resolve_claims`) keeps
its meaning.

What makes promotion workable at tiny support — where a free least-squares fit
is hopeless (sagitta arithmetic: over a 7.5° arc on r = 0.5 the sag is
~0.001 mm against a 2e-5 mm quantum, so a free radius is ~2% ≈ 200× vtol
wrong) — is that **facet normals are exact** and give closed-form,
DOF-reducing initializers:

- **Cylinder — axis = n₁ × n₂.** A planar facet spanning two rulings of a
  cylinder has its normal exactly perpendicular to the axis, at any coarseness,
  so two facets with non-parallel normals give the axis exactly on
  strip/quad-tessellated surfaces. Radius+origin then come from
  `fit_circle_for_axis` (cylindrical.rs), measured well-conditioned on short
  arcs (r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 from 65°/60°/40° arcs). **Provenance
  caveat:** a general Delaunay triangle's normal is *not* exactly
  perpendicular to the axis; measure axis error per provenance (Phase 0's OCCT
  meshes), and degrade to the normal-covariance axis over the earned patch
  where needed — still clean, still not blind.
- **Sphere — concurrent normal lines.** For any planar facet inscribed in a
  sphere, the line through the facet's **circumcenter** along its normal
  passes exactly through the sphere centre (equidistance). Two facets → centre
  at the near-intersection (common-perpendicular midpoint), radius = distance.
- **Cone** — the existing normal-covariance axis (`smallest_eigenvector_3x3`)
  over the earned patch, guarded by `axis_normals_consistent`, both existing.
- **Torus — meridian coplanarity, the "open case" closed.** Every torus
  normal line lies in the meridian plane through its point, which contains
  the axis — so each facet contributes one coplanarity constraint
  `(pᵢ − a) · (nᵢ × d) = 0` on the axis (point a, direction d; 4 DOF; the
  system is bilinear — solve by alternation or pairwise elimination). Four
  facets pin the axis; then (R, r) is a 2-D circle fit in (ρ, z) profile
  coordinates — `fit_circle_2d` already exists (toroidal.rs). **Caveats:**
  rank-deficient by construction on sphere data (any axis through the centre
  satisfies it) and consistent-but-degenerate on cylinder data, so ladder
  order and a conditioning floor must precede it; grid-aligned quads give
  near-exact meridian normals, Delaunay triangles do not. **Time-boxed**: unit
  test to ~1e-9 on synthetic tori before integrating; if conditioning does not
  pan out, skip it — direct torus seeding stays as today and Phase 1 is not
  blocked.

Two behaviours need more than validation failure:

- **Ambiguity → branch, don't choose.** Two same-height faces of a cylinder
  validate as a sphere centred on the axis (the hole→sphere trap at patch
  scale). Grow both branches; the false one should die within a face or two
  (the next face at a different height is off the sphere by ≈ Δ²/2R ≫ vtol).
  Instrument branch lifetime; persistent branches mean validation is less
  sharp than believed — stop and ask.
- **Bent tube → drift-triggered promotion.** A bend is locally a valid
  cylinder (sag of the bend over a 2 mm patch at R = 14 is ~3.6e-5 mm), so
  admission never fails and promotion never triggers on residual. The signal
  is **systematic fit drift**: along a straight tube successive refits are
  stationary; along a bend the fitted axis precesses with a consistent sign as
  support accumulates. Test drift as sign-consistency of successive axis
  deltas, *not* magnitude (a magnitude bar would be a new tuned constant —
  stop and ask if one seems needed). Success retires `promote_bend` and the
  `BEND_*` constants; failure keeps them, and that is fine.

**Deleted when this lands (after ablation evidence):** `condition_patch`
entirely, `seed_patch`'s jet filtering, `SEED_PATCH_CAP`, `MAX_PATCH_DEPTH`,
`PATCH_RADII`, `PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS`, `MIN_CONDITION_FACES`, likely
`MAX_TORUS_STARTS` (multiple flood starts exist because the fit patch was
unreliable).

### Phase 2 design: distance-guided exploration

The 26122-face seed sweep costs 103 s on stepped_block fine and its
useful-work fraction is unknown (profile first — do not build this phase's
machinery if the profile says the cost is in fits or floods rather than the
sweep). The principled orderings and bounds, all derived rather than tuned:

- **Distance field**: per-face geodesic distance to the region boundary
  (multi-source Dijkstra over the dual graph, **centroid-gap weights in
  millimetres, not hops** — hop counts lie on graded meshes, which is what
  `PATCH_RADII` existed to patch). Seed **farthest-first** (Gonzalez maximin),
  replacing `neighbor_jet_mismatch` — the jet-based proxy for exactly this
  quantity.
- **Feedback, not detection**: tangent junctions cannot be *detected* (the
  measured-dead pivot; a tangent junction is locally the *smoothest* place on
  the mesh — an anti-edge), but they are *discovered exactly* by Phase 1's
  excluded frontier faces. Add discovered frontiers as new distance sources
  incrementally. The only trustworthy edge map is the one the fits produce.
- **Duplicate abort**: a seed whose reachable ball lies entirely inside one
  committed surface can only refit that surface — skip it. Topological, a
  strict generalization of `interior_of_claims`, no numeric tolerance.
- **Acceptance discipline**: ordering must not change results. Bit-identical
  probe output, or every diff explained; order-dependence is itself the
  fragility being removed — stop and surface it.

### Phase 0 design: the corpus and the instrument

Three provenance sources, cheapest first, all scored against reference STEPs:

1. **OCCT re-mesh of existing references** (automated, free ground truth).
   `gen_test_models` already drives `BRepMesh` with deflection/angle settings
   (`write_tessellated_stl`); add a mode that loads any reference STEP and
   emits STL variants at several settings, plus **off-origin variants**
   (translate the shape ~1 m before both STL and STEP output) so the float32
   quantum question becomes testable. This adds constrained-Delaunay
   provenance to a strip-heavy corpus.
2. **Fusion 360 re-export** (user batch job): import reference STEPs, export
   STL at low/medium/high. Different tessellator, same ground truth.
3. **Wild STEP+STL pairs** (most representative, most care): harvest models
   published with both a STEP/F3D and an STL (Thingiverse API / Printables /
   GrabCAD, scripted or via a browser session). **Pair-verification gate**:
   run stage 1 `--compare`; a pair counts as ground truth only if mesh
   vertices lie on the STEP surfaces to within a few quanta — otherwise
   quarantine as crash-test-only (the STL may be a different revision,
   decimated, or repaired). Record the verification *rate*: it is itself the
   first field measurement of the provenance premise ("vertices lie on the
   true surface") in the wild. Keep licensing clean: store source URL,
   author, license, and hash in a manifest; do not commit files whose license
   does not permit redistribution (gitignore the external corpus, commit the
   manifest).

The instrument: a one-command sweep harness (per-model rows: per-type surface
breakdown, `--compare` distances, volume error, runtime, exit status;
machine-readable; saved baselines + diff mode) and a **fragility meter** that
perturbs/ablates each stage-2 constant and reports which models change.

### Tolerance sequencing (Phases 0 and 3)

Estimators are built and *reported* in Phase 0 (they double as the
provenance-violation detector: estimator disagreement or a missing dihedral
gap means a decimated/remeshed/faceted-by-construction input — warn now,
degrade gracefully in Phase 6). The **default flip is deferred to Phase 3**,
after the seeding rework stabilizes: a 400× change to `stol` moves behaviour
everywhere (`REFIT_GIVE_UP` is 8×stol; merge gates and `face_validates` use
it), and debugging it concurrently with new seeding would confound both. The
earn loop admits on *vertex* tolerance, so Phase 1 is essentially independent
of the stol regime. Ablation (Phase 4) runs after the flip so deletions are
judged under the final regime.

### Considered and not adopted (with reasons)

- **Recursive subdivision / split-and-merge as the architecture.** Strong
  asymptotics (the tree visits only boundary-containing paths) and its merge
  half already exists (`fit_validate` as union-revalidation), but: fixed
  geometric cuts do not respect seams (boundaries are mesh-edge chains;
  strip-shaped surfaces stay contaminated at every level until cells are
  smaller than the strip), and identifiability *inverts* — a sub-node of a
  real torus legitimately sweeps 5° and fits garbage parameters that validate
  locally, so every sweep guard would need to become "defer, don't reject"
  until merge time, a rework of commit semantics. Phases 1–2 deliver most of
  its benefit at a fraction of the risk. **Held in reserve**; do not start
  without Phase 1–4 measurements in hand and user sign-off.
- **Precomputed junction-evidence field for seeding** (dihedral
  second-difference, diffused; or any jet/dihedral edge map). Any field
  computed *before* fitting is a detector prior — the refuted detector
  returning in a hat. Superseded by the exact, threshold-free fit-derived
  frontier field of Phase 2. (For the record, the raw idea also had a real
  basis: within a tessellated surface the dihedral field is locally
  consistent and *steps* at junctions — but the OCCT-Delaunay and
  coarse-mesh cases erode it, and nothing needs it once frontiers exist.)
- **The "bridge" formulation of type promotion** (grow under an
  exactly-determined uncommittable fit until the new DOF identify). Correct
  shape, but its fragile middle regime — junction faces soaking into
  exactly-determined fits — is mostly obviated by the closed-form
  initializers, which pin the new DOF geometrically at 2–4 faces instead of
  waiting for least-squares identifiability. The earn loop keeps the bridge's
  two sound rules: full-support revalidation on every accretion, and
  identifiability-gated *commits*.
- **VSA (the old item C)** as a planned phase. Earned patches + the existing
  relabel/refit fixpoint already implement the alternation locally, and the
  known-working machinery it would replace (`resolve_claims`, relabel, merge)
  is exactly what a big rewrite puts at risk. Reserve, same gate as
  subdivision. The pending `snap_boundaries` keep/delete decision folds into
  Phase 4 with fresh ablation data (it is the per-face assignment criterion a
  future VSA would need, and the locus diagnostic depends on the module).
- **Retuning anything to close the last corpus gaps first** (stepped fine's 4
  surplus surfaces, medium's ~13). Deliberately deferred: they are boundary-
  and identifiability-residue that Phases 1–4 either fix or isolate, and
  chasing them now re-enters the one-model-at-a-time loop that produced the
  fragility.

### Preserved measurements from retired TODO items

(Everything here is measured; do not re-derive. Sources: 2026-07-26..29
sessions.)

- **Sagitta bound**: no local patch determines a free surface of revolution —
  7.5° arc on r = 0.5 → sag ~0.001 mm vs 2e-5 mm quantum → r ~2% (≈200×
  vtol) wrong. Only DOF reduction helps at small support.
- **Blind-patch facts**: `SEED_PATCH_CAP = 1` collapses stepped_block fine to
  24872 surfaces (the blind patch was the free path's only bootstrap);
  0 of 200 sampled flat seeds produced a patch that stayed on the flat
  (median 50% fillet contamination); 72 of 75 flat components are under the
  32-face cap; a 3232 mm² plane is 12 faces.
- **Borrowed axes**: per-seed borrowed-axis candidates fragment surfaces
  (75 → 100, cylinders 38 → 63, measured twice); (d2) works *only* because it
  selects largest validated area globally before committing. Any borrowed-axis
  use outside (d2) must keep that global selection.
- **Jet reliability**: fine meshes good (κ₁ = 2.005 vs true 2.0); coarse
  garbage (κ₁ = 0.023 / 0.162 / 2.2 across one exact r = 0.5 arc on medium).
  Facet long-edge direction is right ~65% on strips (up to 76° off) —
  corroborating only, never load-bearing.
- **Identifiability, not residual**: impostor cylinders/tori sweep 2.8–6.7°
  vs real surfaces ≥ 64.8°; spurious exactly-determined tori fit at
  0.52/0.72/0.87 quanta vs genuine mean 0.39 / max 0.56 — overlapping, so no
  residual or coverage test rejects them. `candidate_sweep` is vacuous for
  spheres and true cones — a normal-spread identifiability test for those is
  still missing (Phase 5c territory).
- **Exact-normal component exploration** (partial, superseded as a detection
  path): connected components over near-zero-dihedral edges give 342
  components ≥ 10 mm² where the reference has 16 planes, threshold-insensitive
  from 0.001° to 0.2°; top ~16 sum to 14301 mm² vs expected 13783. The
  unexplained tail was suspected large-radius fillets with sub-threshold
  steps. Do not resume as detection; frontier feedback replaces it.
- **hill_with_hole residue**: one junk torus — 17 faces (one over
  `SMALL_SELECTION_ABSORB_FACES = 16`), R = 3.5101 r = 2.3882 (r/R = 0.680,
  just under the 0.7 cap), area 1.533 mm² = **0.095%** of its freeform patch —
  vs `cone_tangent_planes`' real cones at large fractions (1081/163 mm²).
  Relative area is the one untried candidacy quantity. With the decomposition
  forced correct to 7 surfaces it then fails stage 4.1 volume at 6.96e-2
  relative — a separate freeform-accuracy/export issue.
- **Freeform fit failures**: `bspline_blister`'s 926-face component rejected
  by the §2.7 pole-bound check ("control points 2749.5 mm outside the data
  bounding box"), saddle's three components likewise; the check is right, the
  fit conditioning is the bug; nothing recovers, regions fall to per-facet.
  When they do decompose, 4 and 3 invalid micro-sliver faces remain (ignored

- **Sag truth values** for estimator validation: stage-1 `--compare` centroid
  max ≈ 8.9e-4 mm (stepped fine), 9.5e-4 mm (rounded_cube fine); Onshape
  angular settings 12.5°/6.25°/2.5°; Onshape-coarse worst sag 0.026 mm,
  Fusion-low 0.038 mm.
- **cone_15x20**: its reference STEP is itself faceted (59 planes, not
  analytic) — a corpus-data defect, not a pipeline bug; regenerate or mark
  unusable in Phase 0.

### Map from the retired TODO to the new plan

- Runtime bound (old item 2) → Phase 2 (profile first).
- stepped fine surplus / medium gap (old item 3) → Phase 1 acceptance targets,
  then Phase 4/5 residue.
- NURBS corpus blockers → Phase 5a (blister/saddle fit), Phase 5c

- B-prime (quantum) → Phase 0 clamp+report, Phase 5b unit-of-quality.
- B (tolerance estimation) → Phase 0 diagnostics + Phase 3 flip.
- C (VSA) and recursive subdivision → Reserve.
- Step 5 (delete the blind patch) → Phase 1 (earned patches are the missing
  bootstrap design).
- snap_boundaries decision → Phase 4 with fresh ablation data.
- Faceted-by-construction / OpenSCAD → Phase 6.
- 3DBenchy, nosecone coverage, stage-1-only audit → Phase 0.
- Radius-refinement (old "robust rather than tuned" item 3) → Phase 4
  diagnostic (radius clouds should collapse with clean supports; if not,
  investigate support disagreement — never clustering, never snapping).
